   + export control
   + CFR, Code of Federal Regulations, 15 CFR 742.6, part 742, part 740, part 734, supplement no. 5 to part 740, supplement no. 5
   + The Code of Federal Regulations is the standing text of US rules, sorted into titles and parts. Title 15 holds the Export Administration Regulations. Part 734 says what is subject to them. Part 740 lists the license exceptions, and its supplement no. 5 lists the nineteen destinations eligible for License Exception AIA. Part 742 sets the licensing policy for each control.
   + A rule announced in the Federal Register stays in the CFR until it is removed, so the unenforced AI Diffusion rule still stands in the regulations.
   + Read it in Who Controls the Chain Also in Memory and HBM
   + Read it in Who Controls the Chain Also in Memory and HBM

   + 300-millimeter, 12-inch wafer, 12-inch wafers, 12-inch equivalent, 200 mm, 8-inch, 8-inch-equivalent, 450 mm, 150 mm
   + 300 mm is the diameter of the standard wafer, about twelve inches. Older fabs run 200 mm, or 8-inch, wafers. The last increase in wafer size, to 300 mm, came in 2002.
   + Advanced chips start on 300 mm wafers. In SEMI's November 2020 World Fab Forecast, which CSET cited in January 2021, they carried 99.7 percent of world fab capacity at 45 nm and below.
   + Read it in Silicon and Wafers Also in Metrology and Inspection, Transistors and the Front End, Foundries and Fabs, Test and Assembly, The Economics of AI Chips
   + Where it appears, 30 places
   + Where it appears, 30 places
   + 3A090.a, 3A090.b, 3A090.c, 4A090, 4A090.a, ECCN 3A090, ECCN 4A090
   + 3A090 is the entry in the US export control list that covers advanced AI chips, defined by their total processing performance and performance density. Its part c, added in December 2024, covers high bandwidth memory. 4A090 covers the computers built from the chips. A chip that falls under 3A090.a needs a license for China. The AI Diffusion rule of January 2025 extended that requirement to every destination. But BIS says it enforces the requirement only for Country Groups D:1, D:4 and D:5 and for firms headquartered in D:5 or Macau.
   + It decides which AI chips need a license for China, so designers now build chips around its thresholds.
   + Where it appears, 20 places
   + Where it appears, 20 places
   + Where it appears, 20 places
   + Where it appears, 20 places
   + 3B090, 3B001.c.1, 3B001.d.9, 3B001.d.10, 3B001.d.5, 3D991, 3E991
   + 3B001 is the main entry in the US export control list for chipmaking equipment. It is written as process specifications, such as an etch tool that cuts sideways or a deposition tool that coats holes more than 200 times deeper than they are wide. BIS created 3B090 in October 2022 and folded it into 3B001 and 3B002 in October 2023 (88 FR 73424). The related 3D and 3E entries cover the software and know-how that go with the tools.
   + It decides which chipmaking tools need an export license. It defines a tool by the process it can run, because the same chamber can add or remove material depending on its recipe.
   + Read it in Who Controls the Chain Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Deposition and Etch

   + 47 entries
   + Ajinomoto Build-up Film, build-up film, ABF build-up film, ABF substrate, ABF substrates
   + The insulating film inside the substrate, the small layered board that the chips in a high-end package sit on. The board is built up in layers, and a sheet of this epoxy film, about 10 micrometers thick, separates each layer of copper wiring from the next. The Japanese food company Ajinomoto first developed it in 1999.
   + Ajinomoto told investors in June 2026 that its film has held over 95 percent of its market since its launch. The company says practically all high-performance computers and servers rely on it.
   + Read it in Substrates and PCBs
   + Read it in Substrates and PCBs
   + Read it in Substrates and PCBs

   + Contested · Substrates & PCBs · Glass core substrates
   + A subsidiary of Korea's SKC with a final CHIPS Act award of up to $75 million for a 120,000-square-foot glass core substrate plant in Covington, Georgia. As of October 2026, NIST's project page expected the plant's production capacity to come online in 2027. NIST says the glass substrates allow smaller, more densely packed and shorter connections, for faster and more energy-efficient computing.

   + AI accelerator, XPU, accelerators
   + A chip built to do the one kind of arithmetic machine learning needs, multiplying large grids of numbers, far faster than a general-purpose processor can. Nvidia's graphics processors, Google's Tensor Processing Units and Amazon's Trainium are all accelerators.
   + The whole supply chain exists to make accelerators, and export licenses are granted or refused for them.
   + In Chip Design, EDA and IP, Photomasks and Pellicles, Metrology and Inspection, Memory and HBM, Advanced Packaging, Test and Assembly, Systems and Networking, The Economics of AI Chips

   + Important · Interconnect · White-box switch manufacturing
   + A leading contract maker of white-box data center switches, building the hardware that hyperscalers run their own network software on. Accton and its peers build most 800G switch boxes.

   + Contested · Deposition & etch · Wafer cleaning, plating and packaging tools
   + Makes single-wafer cleaning systems using megasonic and bubble technologies, plus electroplating and advanced packaging tools. It is US-listed but manufactures and sells largely in China.

   + Important · Photoresist · ALD and CVD precursor chemicals
   + Makes the organometallic precursor molecules that atomic layer deposition tools use to build films one atomic layer at a time, including metal films and the high-k insulating layers in transistor gates.
   + Important · Equipment subsystems · Plasma power delivery and precision power

   + 2.5D packaging, 3D packaging, 2.5D, 2.5D work, advanced packaging plants
   + The steps that assemble several finished chips into one component, sitting side by side or stacked, wired together closely enough to behave like a single larger chip. Ordinary packaging only puts one chip into a protective case with contacts on the bottom.
   + A modern AI accelerator needs more circuitry than a lithography machine can print in one exposure, and its memory must sit beside the logic, so it is assembled this way. Epoch AI found that TSMC's CoWoS packaging and the memory itself were the main limits on AI chip production in 2025.
   + Read it in Advanced Packaging Also in Lithography, Metrology and Inspection, Memory and HBM, Test and Assembly, The Economics of AI Chips, Who Controls the Chain

   + affiliates rule, 50 percent rule, affiliates of listed entities
   + The Affiliates Rule extended the Entity List to any company anywhere in the world that is at least half owned by a listed firm. The aim was to stop a listed company from buying through a subsidiary. It took effect on 29 September 2025 and was suspended for a year six weeks later.
   + It is suspended until 9 November 2026. If it returns, subsidiaries at least half owned by listed firms will again need licenses.

   + AI Diffusion, diffusion rule, Framework for Artificial Intelligence Diffusion, AI Diffusion framework
   + The AI Diffusion rule, published on 15 January 2025, required a license to ship the most capable AI chips anywhere in the world. Its exceptions allowed unlimited shipments to nineteen destinations, capped volumes elsewhere and none to China. It also controlled the weights of frontier AI models.
   + Four months later BIS said it would not enforce the rule. But it withdrew none of the text, so the license requirement and exceptions still stand in the regulations. BIS says it enforces the requirement only for Country Groups D:1, D:4 and D:5 and for firms headquartered in D:5 or Macau.

   + atomic layer deposition
   + A way of coating a chip a fraction of an atomic layer at a time. Each pulse of gas sticks to the surface and stops once the surface is covered. So the film comes out the same thickness on the floor and on the walls of a hole more than 50 times deeper than it is wide.
   + US regulators call atomic layer deposition an enabler of stacked memory and of FinFET and gate-all-around logic, and export rules name some of its tools directly. Chinese toolmakers held 2.6 percent of the market in 2025 on CSET's count, most of it from Piotech.

   + Important · Deposition & etch · Deposition, epitaxy, CMP, implant and e-beam metrology

   + argon fluoride, argon fluoride laser, 193 nm, 193-nanometer
   + ArF stands for argon fluoride, the gas mixture inside the laser that gives deep ultraviolet lithography its 193-nanometer light. Scanners, the machines that print each layer, use this light in two ways. A dry scanner shines it through air, and an immersion scanner through a thin film of water between lens and wafer, which prints finer lines. ArF scanners print most of the fine layers on any chip made without extreme ultraviolet.
   + ArF immersion scanners made up 42 percent of ASML's system sales in 2025. They are also the kind that a Chinese state-owned firm was reported in July 2026 to be producing at home.

   + back end of line, BEOL, back-end, back-end fabs, back-end tools
   + Inside a fab, the back end of line is the second half of making a chip, the dozen or more layers of copper wiring stacked above the transistors. In the industry at large, the back end means everything after the wafer leaves the fab: grinding, dicing, packaging and test.
   + Wiring is now as hard to make as the transistor. Among the firms that package and test chips after they leave the fab, four of the ten largest by 2024 revenue are headquartered in China, in a TrendForce ranking cited by CSET.
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End Also in Advanced Packaging, Test and Assembly

   + backside power, backside vias, PowerVia, Super Power Rail, backside power network
   + Backside power delivery moves a chip's power wiring to the underside of the wafer, so the wiring above the transistors carries only signals. After the transistors and signal wiring are built, the wafer is bonded face down to a second wafer and ground thin, and the power wires are built on its back. Power arrives through tiny holes etched up from below, clear of the crowded top.
   + It is one of the few remaining ways to make a chip faster without shrinking the transistor. Intel ships it on its 18A process. TSMC says A16, its first process with it, will be production-ready in the second half of 2026.
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End Also in Deposition and Etch
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End Also in Deposition and Etch

   + base chip, base logic die, logic base die, HBM base die
   + The base die is the bottom chip of an HBM stack, the one that manages the memory dies above it and talks to the processor on their behalf. For HBM4, SK hynix moved it to TSMC's advanced logic process so it can hold more functions. Samsung's HBM4, shipped from February 2026, uses a 4 nm logic base die.
   + SK hynix made its own base dies up to HBM3E, so the move gives TSMC a role inside a product it does not make.
   + Read it in Memory and HBM Also in Advanced Packaging
   + Read it in Memory and HBM Also in Advanced Packaging
   + baseboards, SXM, OAM, HGX, HGX baseboard
   + A baseboard is the large circuit board that eight accelerator modules bolt onto inside a server. It carries their power and the links between them. On Nvidia's baseboard, switch chips let any two modules reach each other at full speed. Nvidia's module is called SXM and its baseboard HGX. The open-standard equivalents are OAM and UBB.
   + With an eight-module baseboard, the eight accelerators in one server work as one unit. Rack-scale systems such as the NVL72 drop the baseboard and join 72 GPUs through switch trays in the rack.
   + Read it in Systems and Networking

   + Important · Advanced packaging · Die bonding and hybrid bonding equipment
   + Makes die attach, flip chip and thermocompression bonders, and leads in hybrid bonding tools that join dies copper pad to copper pad without solder. Hybrid bonding is how logic dies are stacked in TSMC's SoIC and future HBM.
   + ball grid array, FC-BGA, flip-chip BGA
   + A way of joining a packaged chip to a circuit board using a grid of small solder balls across the underside in place of a row of pins around the edge. Heating the assembly melts every ball at once into a permanent joint.
   + The number and spacing of those balls limit how much current and how many signals can reach a package.
   + National Integrated Circuit Industry Investment Fund, National Integrated Circuit Fund, third chip fund, China's third chip fund
   + The Big Fund is the nickname for China's National Integrated Circuit Industry Investment Fund, the state vehicle that puts government money into Chinese chip firms. Its third round, raised in May 2024, came to 344 billion yuan, about $47 billion.
   + CSIS puts China's cumulative state funding for chips since 2014 at an estimated $150 billion. The money has built a domestic tool industry but no leading-edge node.
   + Read it in Who Controls the Chain Also in Transistors and the Front End
   + BOM
   + The itemized list of every purchased part in a finished product and what each part cost, the way a builder's invoice prices lumber, wiring and fixtures separately.
   + A bill of materials shows which suppliers get the money a buyer spends. Epoch AI estimated in December 2025 that memory made up about half of the roughly $6,400 manufacturing cost of an Nvidia B200.
   + Read it in The Economics of AI Chips

   + burn in, wafer-level burn-in, burn-in test
   + Burn-in runs a chip hot and at raised voltage for minutes to days, so that any part that would fail early fails in the factory before it is shipped. It can run on the wafer or in the package. Aehr, a maker of burn-in systems, says its lead AI customer is moving all burn-in for its accelerators from the system level to the wafer.
   + One weak die in a package of a dozen expensive parts scraps all of them, so burn-in on the wafer protects everything else in the package.
   + busbars, copper busbar, copper busbars
   + A busbar is a thick bar of solid copper that carries large currents down the back of a rack to the boards that need them. An ordinary cable carrying the same current would overheat. At the 54 volts used inside racks such as the GB300 NVL72, a one-megawatt rack would need up to 200 kilograms of busbar, by Nvidia's estimate.
   + Nvidia is moving its racks to 800 volts, which cuts the current, so a megawatt rack will not need bars that heavy.

   + case by case, case-by-case, reviewed case by case
   + Case-by-case review means a license is required and each application is judged on its own, with no presumption either way. Since January 2026 Nvidia's H200 and AMD's MI325X have been reviewed this way for China, with conditions attached to each approval.
   + It is the middle ground between an open market and a presumption of denial. In early 2026 the United States settled on it for chips such as the H200 bound for China.

   + chips, microchip, microchips, semiconductor chip
   + A chip is a small rectangle of silicon, usually smaller than a fingernail, that carries a complete electronic circuit, often made of billions of transistors. It is cut from a wafer after the circuit has been built up on it layer by layer.
   + The supply chain is every firm and country that takes part in making chips, and some of them can stop production.
   + Read it in Silicon and Wafers Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist, Lithography, Photomasks and Pellicles, Deposition and Etch, Metrology and Inspection, Transistors and the Front End, Foundries and Fabs, Memory and HBM, Advanced Packaging, Substrates and PCBs, Test and Assembly, Systems and Networking, Data Centers and Power, The Economics of AI Chips, Who Controls the Chain

   + chiplets
   + One of several smaller pieces that a chip has been deliberately split into, each manufactured separately and then wired back together inside a single package. A chip built this way is like a house assembled from prefabricated rooms.
   + Many large accelerators need more circuitry than a lithography machine can print in one exposure, so they are built from chiplets. That makes packaging, the step that joins the chiplets, the hard part. Epoch AI found that TSMC's CoWoS packaging and memory were the main limits on AI chip production in 2025.

   + The CHIPS and Science Act authorized about $52.7 billion of US federal money for the chip industry. By July 2025 the Commerce Department had awarded $30.9 billion of direct funding to 19 companies for 40 projects.

   + chokepoints
   + A step in the supply chain where so few firms or countries can do the work that losing one of them stops everybody.
   + One sanction or one factory fire at a chokepoint reaches the whole industry, and so does a government policy aimed at one.
   + In Lithography, Deposition and Etch, Who Controls the Chain
   + Important · Test · Test instruments, handlers and system-level test
   + Makes automated test equipment, power test instruments and system-level test (SLT) platforms, including the burn-in and SLT systems used for AI processors and power components.
   + Diversified · Interconnect · Networking systems and Silicon One switch chips
   + Sells data center switches and routers and designs its own Silicon One switch chips, giving it a second in-house alternative to switch chips bought from chip vendors for AI networks.
   + complementary metal-oxide-semiconductor, complementary MOS
   + CMOS, short for complementary metal-oxide-semiconductor, is the standard way of building digital circuits from pairs of transistors. Each n-type transistor, which switches on when the voltage on its gate is high, is matched with a p-type transistor, which switches on when that voltage is low. In either logic state one side of the circuit is off, so little current flows except while it switches.
   + CMOS became the dominant technology for making chips because its circuits drew close to zero power while idle, which kept heat in check as chips packed in more transistors. FinFET and gate-all-around transistors are newer shapes for the same n-type and p-type pairs.
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End

   + chemical mechanical polishing, chemical mechanical planarization, polishing slurry, polishing slurries, slurry, slurries, CMP slurry, CMP pads, planarization
   + Chemical mechanical polishing, or CMP, rubs a wafer's surface flat again after each new layer goes on, using a pad and a liquid full of fine grit called slurry. Without it the next layer would be printed on a surface too bumpy to focus on.
   + Japanese companies held 51 percent of polishing-slurry sales in 2023, counted by company nationality. CSET's January 2021 brief, drawing on 2019 market reports, put DuPont and Cabot Microelectronics at about 56 percent of a $790 million slurry market. Cabot was renamed CMC Materials in October 2020, and Entegris bought it in July 2022. DuPont's electronics business became Qnity Electronics in November 2025.
   + Read it in Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist Also in Photomasks and Pellicles, Advanced Packaging
   + co-packaged, co-packaging, CPO, plug-in modules, plug-in optics
   + Co-packaged optics puts the parts that turn electrical signals into light and back onto the switch chip's own package instead of into plug-in modules at the front of the box. Broadcom keeps the lasers that supply the light in separate modules that can be replaced in the field.
   + Broadcom and Nvidia are moving the optics onto the switch package partly to cut the power the optics use. Broadcom says its Tomahawk 6 Davisson cuts optical interconnect power by 70 percent.
   + Read it in Systems and Networking Also in Memory and HBM
   + coater-developer, coater and developer, coater/developer track, track tool, track tools
   + A coater/developer, or track, is the machine that spins a thin coat of photoresist onto the wafer before it enters the scanner. After the wafer comes out, the same machine washes away the exposed parts, so every printed layer passes through it twice.
   + No pattern can be printed without it. Tokyo Electron's own results put its share of the market at 91 percent in 2025.
   + CTE, thermal expansion, expansion mismatch, low-CTE
   + The coefficient of thermal expansion is how much a material expands for each degree it warms. Silicon barely expands and epoxy expands more, so when the two are bonded and heated, the package bends.
   + Warpage control, keeping a package from bending, means matching expansion across die, bridge, interposer and substrate. It gets harder as packages grow.

   + Important · Equipment subsystems · RF impedance matching and X-ray inspection
   + Makes RF power delivery components, vacuum capacitors and impedance matching networks for plasma tools, and X-ray systems used to inspect solder joints and packages.
   + training compute threshold
   + A limit set on the amount of arithmetic used to train an AI model. It counts operations, whichever chips run them. The AI Diffusion rule of January 2025 made one an export control. ECCN 4E091 covers "'Parameters' for an artificial intelligence model trained utilizing 10^26 or more 'operations'". The rules require a license to send such parameters to every destination worldwide (15 CFR 742.6(a)(13)). Article 51(2) of the EU AI Act sets Europe's line ten times lower. It presumes a model has high-impact capabilities when the cumulative computation used for training is "greater than 10^25" floating point operations.
   + Every other control threshold measures a chip, but this one measures the model the chips train. Article 51(3) of the AI Act lets the European Commission change the EU figure by delegated act. Such an act takes effect unless the European Parliament or the Council objects within three months (Article 97(6)). BIS said in May 2025 that it would not enforce the AI Diffusion rule, but 4E091 still stands in the regulations.
   + concentration band
   + A four-band judgment of how many firms could credibly supply a stage today, taken at the narrowest step inside it. Extreme means three or fewer, or one firm holding more than half the stage. High means four to six, with the top two taking most of the revenue. Moderate means seven or more, none of them dominant. Low means many, with the scarce input something another industry sells at volume. Where a figure exists for the top country's share of the segment, it is a second check. When the two disagree, the supplier count wins.
   + The fewer the firms that can do a stage, the more one accident or one license decision affects the whole chain.
   + In Who Controls the Chain
   + consignees, named consignee, named consignees, ultimate consignee
   + The consignee is the party a shipment is sent to, the name on the export license. A named consignee, such as G42 in the United Arab Emirates, can receive controlled chips on the terms attached to its own name.
   + The July 2026 UAE rule names the Emirati government, G42 and eight US-headquartered AI companies as consignees who can take controlled AI chips without a license. The license-free treatment is tied to those named buyers.

   + Makes the optical fiber and cabling that connect data center halls and campuses, and develops glass substrates for semiconductor packaging. It also holds the majority stake in Hemlock Semiconductor.
   + Country Group, Country Group D:5, D:5, A:5, D:1, D:3, D:4, arms-embargoed countries
   + The groups that US export rules sort the world's countries into, published as supplement no. 1 to 15 CFR part 740. A:5 and A:6 list close partners. D:1, D:4 and D:5 are the national security, missile technology and arms-embargoed groups. E:1 and E:2 are the most restricted. A country can be in several groups at once, and the license requirement usually depends on which.
   + US license rules for AI chips are written in terms of these groups. Chips in the 3A090.b band, the band below the top one, need a license for D:1, D:4 and D:5 destinations not also in A:5 or A:6 (15 CFR 742.6(a)(6)(iii)(B)). The UAE moved from D:3 and D:4 into A:5 in July 2026. But the rule names the UAE, so these chips still need a license there, except for approved Emirati consignees (15 CFR 742.6(a)(6)(iii)(B)).

   + chip on wafer on substrate
   + TSMC's method for mounting logic chips and memory stacks side by side on a shared interposer, a layer of fine wiring, then mounting that interposer on a rigid base board. In CoWoS-S the interposer is one piece of silicon. In CoWoS-L, used for Nvidia's Blackwell and Rubin, it is layers of fine copper wiring with small embedded silicon bridges. Nearly every high-end AI accelerator is built this way.
   + Epoch AI found that TSMC's CoWoS packaging and memory were the main limits on AI chip production in 2025. It estimates that Nvidia used 60.3 percent of all CoWoS that year. SemiAnalysis judged in March 2026 that CoWoS supply was easing, but in July 2026 TSMC said its packaging capacity was so tight that it limited customers' growth.

   + Important · Interconnect · SerDes, active electrical cables and optical DSPs
   + Supplies high-speed SerDes IP, active electrical cables and retimers used for 400G and 800G links inside and between AI racks. Its active electrical cables are copper and carry signals up to 7 meters between neighboring racks, replacing optics over those distances at lower power.
   + critical dimensions, CD, CD-SEM, critical-dimension, line width, line-width, line widths
   + The critical dimension is the width of the finest printed feature on a layer. A CD-SEM is an electron microscope built to measure it across a wafer without cutting anything.
   + Fabs measure it to tell whether each lithography and etch step came out the right width. In 2025 Hitachi held 71.9 percent of the market for the tools that measure it, by CSET's count. They are the one kind of inspection or measurement tool that Japan leads.

   + Center for Strategic and International Studies

   + chemical vapor deposition, PECVD, plasma-enhanced chemical vapor deposition, MOCVD, CVD precursor, precursor, precursors
   + Chemical vapor deposition, or CVD, grows a film by flowing gases over a hot wafer so they react on its surface and leave a solid layer behind. The plasma-enhanced version, PECVD, runs cooler. The gases used are called precursors.
   + CVD made up $11.2 billion of a $26.2 billion deposition tool market in 2025, most of it the plasma-enhanced kind. Chinese toolmakers' share of the deposition tool market rose from 2 to 7 percent between 2019 and 2024.
   + Contested · Memory · Chinese DRAM and early HBM
   + China's only significant DRAM maker, producing DDR4 and DDR5 and working toward HBM. It has scaled capacity quickly enough to move commodity DRAM prices.
   + Critical · Lithography · DUV excimer light sources and EUV tin plasma sources
   + Builds the light sources for lithography: excimer lasers for DUV scanners and the laser-produced tin plasma source that generates 13.5 nm EUV light. The EUV source is the hardest subsystem in the machine.

   + Contested · Accelerator vendors · Digital in-memory compute inference accelerators
   + Builds inference accelerators that compute inside SRAM, which cuts the energy spent moving model weights. They are aimed at batch inference, which runs many requests to a large language model at once.
   + Important · Photomasks · Photomasks and lead frames
   + Makes photomasks, including EUV masks, plus lead frames and other electronic components. Like Toppan, it applied the precision of commercial printing to semiconductor patterning.
   + The share of controlled American content a foreign-made item may contain before US export rules, the EAR, apply to it. Under 15 CFR 734.4 the general threshold is 25 percent of the item's value, and 10 percent for the most restricted destinations. Above the threshold the item falls under the EAR, and below it the item does not.
   + It decides whether foreign-made goods with some US content fall under American rules. The foreign direct product rules work differently and catch an item whatever its share of US content. Under 15 CFR 734.4(a)(3), equipment in ECCN 3B993.f.1 has no de minimis level when it is bound for advanced-node chip production. The exception is equipment first exported from a country that also controls it.
   + D0, defects per square centimeter
   + The average number of chip-killing flaws per square centimeter of processed wafer. It is a fab's quality score, inferred from how many chips come out working.
   + AI accelerators have some of the largest dies in production, and yield, the share of dies that work, falls steeply as die area times defect density grows. In a textbook example at 0.8 defects per square centimeter, 49.2 percent of dies 1 centimeter on a side come out flawless, against 11.3 percent of dies 2 centimeters on a side. The share that can be sold is higher on chips that carry spare blocks.
   + Diversified · Systems & servers · AI servers and integrated systems
   + Sells PowerEdge AI servers and integrated rack systems to enterprises and AI cloud operators, adding services, financing and deployment on top of the hardware.
   + Important · Power & cooling · Power supplies, power shelves and thermal systems
   + A global leader in switching power supplies and a maker of the power shelves and busbars used in AI racks, plus fans, cold plates and coolant distribution units. It supplies equipment for the 800-volt DC power architecture that Nvidia has moved toward.
   + An order from BIS that shuts a named party out of trade in US-controlled items, as a sanction or a protective measure, with no license policy involved. Under 15 CFR 764.3 it restricts the party's exports, reexports and in-country transfers of items subject to the EAR, and its access to them. It can also suspend or revoke the party's outstanding licenses. There is no application to file. Orders can be issued outright or suspended and later activated.
   + It can cut a named party off from every item subject to the EAR. The order activated against ZTE in April 2018 stayed in force until BIS terminated it 89 days later as part of a settlement (83 FR 17644; 83 FR 34825).
   + Adding a layer of material to a wafer. Machines knock atoms off a solid target so they land on the surface, react gases at the surface so a film grows there, or plate metal out of a liquid. It is how the insulators, the wires and the parts of each transistor are built up.
   + Making one chip takes 1,000 to 1,500 steps by a 2025 industry estimate, and many of them are deposition and etch. AI hardware needs films that reach the bottom of deeper holes. Four firms make most of the tools, but deposition is among the tool types where Chinese makers gained the most share between 2019 and 2024.

   + wafer dicing, dicing saw, dicing saws, grinding, wafer grinding, grinders, wafer grinders, thinning, wafer thinning
   + Dicing cuts a finished wafer into its individual dies with a diamond saw or a laser. Grinding, done first, thins the wafer from the back, and for stacked memory it goes on until the copper columns inside the dies come through.
   + Stacked high bandwidth memory (HBM) depends on that grinding. Disco of Japan held 87 percent of the world market for dicing saws and wafer grinders in 2025, by CSET's count.
   + Read it in Test and Assembly Also in Advanced Packaging
   + Read it in Test and Assembly Also in Advanced Packaging
   + singular of dice, dies
   + One rectangular chip cut out of a wafer. Hundreds are printed side by side on the same disc and then sliced apart, and each separated rectangle is a die.
   + Cost and yield are counted per die. A GPU die that fills the largest area one exposure can print loses far more silicon to one fatal defect than a small chip does.
   + Read it in Silicon and Wafers Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Metrology and Inspection, Transistors and the Front End, Memory and HBM, Advanced Packaging, Test and Assembly, The Economics of AI Chips
   + Where it appears, 71 places
   + dielectrics, dielectric etch, dielectric properties, insulator, insulating layer
   + A dielectric is an insulator: a material that does not conduct, such as the glassy oxides and nitrides between a chip's wires or the epoxy in a package substrate. Dielectric etch cuts holes and trenches through those insulating layers so the wires can be laid in.
   + How much signal a dielectric absorbs sets how fast a substrate or circuit board can run. So the suppliers of laminates, the insulating sheets these boards are made from, matter to AI systems.

   + dynamic random-access memory
   + A computer's main working memory. Each bit is a charge held in a microscopic capacitor that leaks, so the chip has to refresh its contents constantly. It forgets everything when the power goes off.
   + HBM is a stack of DRAM chips, and the three leading DRAM makers, SK hynix, Samsung and Micron, make nearly all of it. SK hynix says the shift of production capacity to HBM left ordinary DRAM, the kind in PCs and phones, undersupplied. The company says the shortage helped drive a significant price recovery from the third quarter of 2025.

   + deep ultraviolet, deep-ultraviolet, DUV scanner, DUV scanners, DUV tools
   + Chipmaking light from gas lasers, at 248 or 193 nanometers, and the machines that print with it, which still make up most lithography tools. Filling the gap between lens and wafer with water sharpens the image, a method called immersion.
   + A fab without extreme ultraviolet machines can reach leading-edge logic only with immersion deep ultraviolet and several passes per layer, as SMIC does. So Dutch export licensing covers the most advanced immersion tools.

   + EAR
   + 22 entries
   + Export Administration Regulations
   + The body of US rules that governs the export of commercial goods, software and technology, including items with both civilian and military uses. The Bureau of Industry and Security writes and enforces them. They run from 15 CFR part 730 to part 774. Part 734 sets what they cover, part 742 the license requirements, part 744 the end-use and end-user controls, part 740 the exceptions and part 764 the penalties.
   + American export rules on chips and chipmaking tools are amendments to these regulations, made by notice in the Federal Register with no act of Congress.
   + The label for an item that falls under US export rules but is not named on the Commerce Control List, the catalog of controlled items. The label is defined at 15 CFR 734.3(c). It is a catch-all category, but an EAR99 item still needs a license if it is going to a listed party or a controlled end use.
   + An EAR99 item can usually ship without a license, but a listed buyer, a controlled end use or an embargoed destination can still require one. So exporters of unlisted items have to follow the end-user controls in part 744 of the EAR too.
   + firm, Ireland
   + Important · Power & cooling · Power distribution, switchgear and UPS
   + Makes switchgear, busway, transformers, UPS systems and power distribution units for data centers, a segment that has become supply constrained as AI campuses scale.
   + Export Control Classification Number
   + The catalog number that US export rules assign to a controlled item, similar to a customs tariff code but for security-sensitive technology. Number 3A090 covers advanced computing chips and 3B001 covers chipmaking equipment.
   + An item's classification number determines whether it needs a license. So the technical thresholds written into each number set which products are controlled.
   + Read it in Who Controls the Chain Also in Deposition and Etch, Memory and HBM

   + electronic design automation, chip design software
   + The software that turns a description of what a chip should do into the exact pattern of shapes to be printed on silicon. It also proves the design will work before anyone commits money to masks and wafers.
   + Three vendors sell the only software that covers every step of designing a leading-edge chip. Since October 2022 software made for designing chips with gate-all-around transistors has needed a US export license. In May 2025 the US government sent letters to the three vendors requiring a license for all their sales to China, and it lifted the requirement on 2 July.

   + Important · Equipment subsystems · Dry vacuum pumps and abatement systems
   + Supplies the dry pumps that hold process chambers at vacuum and the abatement systems that destroy the toxic and greenhouse gases leaving them. A leading-edge fab runs thousands of pumps continuously.
   + electron-beam, e-beam, electron beams, multi-beam, multibeam, e-beam inspection, electron-beam metrology
   + An electron beam is a stream of electrons focused to a spot far smaller than any spot of light. Mask shops use it to write the pattern onto a photomask, and fabs use it to inspect wafers for defects light cannot see and to measure line widths. A multi-beam tool fires many beams at once, because a single beam is slow.
   + In 2023 Intel called Austria's IMS Nanofabrication the established leader in the multi-beam writers that advanced EUV masks require. Since December 2024 US rules have controlled wafer inspection tools that use two or more beams to find defects 21 nm across or smaller.
   + Read it in Photomasks and Pellicles Also in Metrology and Inspection
   + Where it appears, 23 places
   + electronic grade, semiconductor-grade, semiconductor grade, solar-grade, solar grade
   + Electronic-grade, or semiconductor-grade, silicon is refined until its metal impurities are no more than a few parts per billion, pure enough for chips. Solar-grade silicon is a less pure grade made for solar cells.
   + Every chip starts from it. The Commerce Department found in 2026 that semiconductor-grade polysilicon makes up only 2.4 percent of global production. The German wafer maker Siltronic names five major suppliers for 2025: Wacker, Hemlock, OCI, Tokuyama and Mitsubishi. None of them is Chinese.

   + end user, end users, end-user, end use, military end user
   + Export rules that depend on who the buyer is and what they will do with the item, whatever the item is. The same tool can ship freely to one customer and need a license that will be refused for another.
   + A company can lose access to US-controlled items because of who owns it or what its site produces. In September 2025 US rules were extended to firms at least 50 percent owned by listed entities, though the extension is suspended until 9 November 2026.

   + Important · Chemicals & gases · Filtration, wafer handling and process chemistries
   + Makes the filters, purifiers, fluid handling components, wafer carriers and specialty coatings that keep contamination out of a fab. It also sells CMP slurries, a business it gained by buying CMC Materials. Fabs use up its products continuously.
   + entity-list, Entity-Listed, Entity List additions, listed entity, listed entities, listed firm, listed firms
   + A published list of foreign companies, institutes and individuals. Anyone anywhere, American or foreign, needs a license to send them an item subject to the EAR. Each entry states its own license review policy. Most carry a presumption of denial, meaning the license will normally be refused. SMIC's entry, as amended in December 2024, is a presumption of denial for all items. The exception is tools designed for 200 mm wafer production and bound for a 200 mm plant, which are reviewed case by case. The list is supplement no. 4 to 15 CFR part 744, and the license requirement is in 15 CFR 744.11.
   + It names an organization, so it is one of the simplest tools the US government has. In September 2025 the US extended each listing to companies at least 50 percent owned by listed firms, but that extension is suspended until 9 November 2026 (90 FR 50857).
   + Important · Optics & photonics · High-speed optical transceivers
   + A major supplier of 400G, 800G and 1.6T optical transceivers to cloud and AI data center operators, and one of the fastest growing Chinese optics firms.
   + epitaxial, epitaxial wafer, epitaxial wafers, epitaxial layer, epi
   + Epitaxy grows a fresh layer of crystal on top of a wafer, atom by atom, so the new layer continues the crystal beneath it with almost no flaws. An epitaxial wafer is a plain wafer with that extra layer, and the transistors are built in it.
   + Flaws in that layer can make the transistors leak current. The industry association SEMI credits the 2025 rise in wafer shipments to AI demand for advanced epitaxial wafers for logic chips and polished wafers for high-bandwidth memory.
   + Read it in Silicon and Wafers Also in Deposition and Etch, Transistors and the Front End
   + Contested · Silicon wafers · 300 mm polished and epitaxial wafers
   + Mainland China's largest maker of 300 mm wafers. It says it ranked sixth in the world with about 7.7 percent of global 300 mm shipments in the first half of 2026, up from about 6 percent in 2024.
   + etching, etched, etches, etcher, etchers, dry etch
   + Removing material from a wafer wherever the printed pattern says it should not be. Most etch tools turn a gas into a plasma whose ions and fragments eat one material and leave the material beside it alone.
   + Wrap-around transistors need etch that cuts sideways while sparing the material beside it. Stacked memory needs holes far deeper than they are wide. So US rules control etch tools by selectivity, meaning how well a tool cuts one material while sparing another, and by hole shape.
   + Read it in Deposition and Etch Also in Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist, Lithography, Photomasks and Pellicles, Metrology and Inspection, Transistors and the Front End, Memory and HBM, Advanced Packaging, Who Controls the Chain

   + Contested · Accelerator vendors · Transformer-specific ASIC
   + Designing Sohu, a custom chip (ASIC) that hard-wires the transformer architecture into silicon. The transformer is the model design behind large language models. Sohu gives up flexibility to do more work per watt.
   + Ultra Ethernet, Spectrum-X, Spectrum-X Ethernet, Tomahawk, Ethernet switch
   + Ethernet is the ordinary networking standard of offices and the internet, now made fast and reliable enough to link the servers of an AI cluster. Its switch chips, such as Broadcom's Tomahawk, route traffic between thousands of accelerators.
   + Broadcom has doubled the bandwidth of its Ethernet switch chips every generation, from 25.6 Tbps on one chip in Tomahawk 4 in 2019 to 102.4 in Tomahawk 6 in 2025.
   + Read it in Systems and Networking Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP
   + extreme ultraviolet, extreme-ultraviolet, EUV scanner, EUV scanners, extreme ultraviolet lithography
   + The light that prints the finest layers of every leading-edge chip, and the machines that print with it. Its wavelength is 13.5 nanometers, and shorter light draws finer lines. Air and glass both absorb it, so the machine works in a vacuum and carries the light on mirrors. The light comes from droplets of molten tin, 50,000 a second, each hit twice by a laser.
   + The finest layers set how densely transistors and wires pack, so the most advanced AI chips depend on EUV. Only ASML makes the machines, and export controls block it from shipping them to Chinese chipmakers.
   + Read it in Lithography Also in Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist, Photomasks and Pellicles, Metrology and Inspection, Transistors and the Front End, Foundries and Fabs, Who Controls the Chain

   + export controls, export licensing, export license, export licenses, export licence, export licences, export-control
   + An export control is a rule that requires the government's permission for a sale. The government names an item and a country or a buyer. From then on its own companies must hold a license before they ship that item to that country or buyer. The United States extends some of these rules to goods made abroad with American technology.
   + It works only when the controlling country's firms make something the buyer cannot get elsewhere.

   + fabrication plant, wafer fab, fabs, chip factory, chip factories
   + The factory where wafers are processed into chips. It is a cleanroom where each wafer moves from machine to machine in a sealed pod for months. The building costs tens of billions of dollars.

   + Where it appears, 77 places
   + A chip company that designs and sells chips but owns no factory, paying a contract manufacturer, a foundry, to make them. Nvidia, AMD, Apple, Qualcomm and Broadcom all work this way.
   + The fabless model concentrates manufacturing in a handful of foundries. So a designer's supply, its costs and where its chips are made all depend on a company it does not control.

   + Important · Optics & photonics · Contract manufacturing of optical modules
   + A contract manufacturer of optical modules and photonic assemblies, building transceivers and optical engines for the largest networking and AI vendors from plants in Thailand.
   + FR, 89 FR, 91 FR, 88 FR, Federal Register notice
   + The Federal Register is the US government's daily journal, where every new rule is published. An export rule can take effect a few days before it is printed there, as the UAE rule did in July 2026. A citation such as 89 FR 96790 means volume 89, page 96790, and is the usual way to cite a rule.
   + US export rules change by notices published there, with no act of Congress needed.
   + Important · Equipment subsystems · Quartz, ceramics, ferrofluidic seals and thermoelectrics
   + Supplies quartz and ceramic chamber consumables, ferrofluidic vacuum feedthroughs, electrostatic chucks and thermoelectric coolers, with much of its manufacturing in China.
   + fin field-effect transistor
   + A transistor whose channel, the strip that current flows through, stands on edge like a shark fin. The gate, the electrode that switches the current, wraps around three sides of the fin instead of resting on one. More contact means firmer control and less leakage when the switch is meant to be off.
   + TSMC's leading-edge processes used FinFETs until N2, its first process with gate-all-around transistors, entered volume production in late 2025. The October 2022 US rules used FinFET or 16/14 nm logic as one of their thresholds for an advanced fab.
   + floating point operations per second, TFLOPS, PFLOPS, FLOP, teraflops
   + A count of how many arithmetic operations on floating-point numbers, numbers with a fractional part, a chip can perform each second, the way horsepower counts an engine's output.
   + It is the standard unit of AI compute, but two chips' figures can be compared only when the number format behind each, such as 8-bit or 16-bit, is stated. US export rules use their own score, TPP, which weights each operation by its bit length.
   + FDPR, FDP rule, foreign-produced direct product rule, direct product rule, foreign-produced item rules, foreign-produced item rule, foreign-produced item
   + A US rule that puts a foreign-made item under American control when two tests are both met. The first is about the product: the item was made directly from specified US technology or software, or in a plant that was. The second is about the destination: the item is going somewhere, or to someone, the rule names. The mechanics are in 15 CFR 734.9.
   + The rule lets US controls reach goods made outside the United States. Without it, a buyer on the Entity List could escape US controls by ordering chips from a supplier outside the United States. With it, a chip made in Taiwan on American tools for Huawei needs a US license. For listed firms the rule reaches only those whose entry carries a footnote naming it, such as Huawei's (15 CFR 734.9(e)).
   + Read it in Who Controls the Chain Also in Deposition and Etch, Metrology and Inspection, Memory and HBM
   + Important · Test · Probe cards and test systems
   + Supplies the MEMS probe cards that make temporary electrical contact with thousands of pads on a wafer during testing, including HBM and logic probe cards. It is a leading probe card maker, with $637.9 million of revenue in its probe card segment in 2025.
   + pure-play foundry, contract chipmaker, foundries, pure-play, pure play, pure-play model, contract chipmaking
   + A company, or a division of one, that manufactures chips designed by other companies. A pure-play foundry, the model TSMC pioneered after its founding in 1987, makes no products of its own. The model works because no chip company sells enough of one product to fill a modern fab on its own.
   + All six of TSMC's largest plants are in Taiwan. Taiwan held 69 percent of the world's logic capacity below 10 nm in 2022, so political events there affect the whole chip supply.

   + 8-bit and 4-bit floating point, FP8, FP4, FP16, BF16, INT8, number formats
   + Ways of writing a number using only 8 or 4 binary digits, or bits, far fewer than standard formats use. Each number is less precise, but the chip can perform and store far more of them. That trade-off suits machine learning, which can work with less exact numbers.
   + Lower precision raises a chip's headline speed, so a performance figure quoted without its number format cannot be compared. US export rules allow for this by weighting each operation by its bit length.

   + front end of line, FEOL, front-end, front-end wafers
   + The front end of line is the first half of making a chip, the steps that build the transistors in the silicon. In the industry at large, the front end means the whole wafer fab, as against the back end where chips are packaged and tested.
   + SemiAnalysis judged in March 2026 that front-end capacity had overtaken packaging as the main bottleneck on AI chip output. But TSMC said in July 2026 that its packaging capacity still limited customers' growth.

   + gallium, germanium, antimony
   + Gallium and germanium are metals used in electronics. Gallium goes into compound chips such as radio parts, LEDs and lasers, and germanium into fiber optics, infrared optics, semiconductors and solar cells. Both are recovered as byproducts, gallium from processing bauxite and germanium from certain zinc ores and coal.
   + The US Geological Survey put China's share of world production of primary low-purity gallium at 99 percent in February 2026. China has required licenses to export both metals since August 2023. Its December 2024 ban on exports of both to the United States is suspended until 27 November 2026.

   + GAA, nanosheet transistor, RibbonFET at Intel, nanosheet, nanosheets, RibbonFET, GAA transistor, gate-all-around transistors
   + A transistor in which the gate, the electrode that switches the current, wraps all the way around the channel that current flows through. In a FinFET, the design it replaces, the gate touches three sides. Surrounding the channel gives the gate firmer control than a fin allows, so less current leaks when the switch is off.
   + Only TSMC, Intel and Samsung make it in volume. It needs etch that cuts sideways, which US export rules control. Customers building 2 nm gate-all-around capacity lifted ASM International, the leader in atomic layer deposition, to record revenue in 2025.

   + TB/s, Gb/s, Tbps, gigabytes per second, terabytes per second, gigabits per second, terabits per second
   + GB/s is gigabytes per second, the measure of how much data a link or a memory can move. TB/s is a thousand times more. A lowercase b, as in Gb/s or Tbps, counts bits, each an eighth of a byte. One HBM4 stack moves over 2 TB/s. NVLink 6 gives each GPU 3,600 GB/s, according to Nvidia's January 2026 Rubin launch.
   + The October 2022 US rule caught chips with a processing score of 4,800 or more and an input and output rate of 600 GB/s or more (87 FR 62186). Since January 2026, chips with total DRAM bandwidth below 6,500 GB/s and a processing score, or TPP, below 21,000 can be licensed case by case for China (91 FR 1684).

   + Critical · Advanced packaging · Thermocompression bonders for HBM
   + Makes the thermocompression bonders used to stack HBM dies, including dual-head models, and supplies SK hynix and Micron.
   + high bandwidth memory, high-bandwidth memory, HBM3E, HBM4, HBM4E, HBM stack, HBM stacks, memory stacks, stacked memory
   + Stacked memory built for AI chips. Up to sixteen memory chips are stacked into a cube and wired together through copper-filled holes made in each chip before stacking. The cube sits a few millimeters from the processor. It holds the model the chip is running, the numbers called weights, and hands them to the processor over 1,024 or more wires at once.
   + To write each word of an answer, the chip reads the whole model from this memory, along with the record of the conversation so far. A larger model or a longer conversation needs more memory, so the size and speed of HBM usually set how many people one chip can serve. HBM is the largest line in the bill of materials of an Nvidia B200. SK hynix, Samsung and Micron make nearly all of it.

   + high numerical aperture extreme ultraviolet, ASML EXE series, High-NA, High NA, High-NA scanners, High-NA machine, High-NA tools
   + The next generation of extreme ultraviolet machine, whose larger mirrors collect light over a wider angle and so print smaller features. The wider optics also halve the area the machine can print in one exposure, so a large chip has to be stitched together from two exposures.
   + It cuts the smallest printable feature from 13 nanometers to 8, and TSMC expects more AI chip layers to need it. Each machine sells for an estimated $350 to $400 million. Intel says it uses High-NA in volume production for select layers of some 18A products. TSMC plans to use it in high-volume manufacturing from 2030.
   + Read it in Lithography Also in Photomasks and Pellicles, Foundries and Fabs
   + HPC, HPC platform, high-performance computers
   + High-performance computing is the industry's name for the heavy end of the market: supercomputers, AI accelerators and the server processors beside them. A foundry's HPC platform is the version of a node tuned for those chips.
   + AI accelerators are part of this segment and need package substrates with more layers than earlier HPC chips. Ajinomoto, which makes the insulating film between those layers, counts 11 layers of it on each side of the core in a 2026 AI package substrate, against nine on each side of an HPC substrate around 2023.
   + Read it in Foundries and Fabs Also in Metrology and Inspection, Substrates and PCBs
   + HVM, volume production, mass production, high volume, in volume, production ramp
   + High-volume manufacturing is the point where a new process or product stops being a trial and is made in volume for sale. Output then climbs to full rate during the ramp.
   + Foundries announce the date a process reaches it, and before then the process runs only in trials such as risk production.

   + direct bond interconnect, SoIC, Foveros Direct, copper pad to copper pad
   + Joining two chips by bonding their copper pads, and the glass-like insulator around them, directly to each other, with no solder in between. Removing the solder lets connections be packed much more tightly and shortens the path heat has to travel out.
   + An SK hynix tech note puts microbumps about 20 micrometers apart and hybrid-bonded pads under 1 micrometer apart. It expects full-scale use in HBM at HBM4E or HBM5, where stacks are projected to pass twenty layers.
   + Read it in Advanced Packaging Also in Memory and HBM, Test and Assembly
   + hydrofluoric acid, HF, ultra-high-purity hydrofluoric acid, high-purity hydrogen fluoride
   + Hydrogen fluoride is a gas that dissolves glass, and hydrofluoric acid is its solution in water. Fabs use both to strip oxide layers and clean wafers between steps. Like other cleaning chemicals, it is purified until metal impurities are down to parts per trillion.
   + When Japan put it under individual export licenses for South Korea in 2019, Japan's exports of it to South Korea fell 87.9 percent. South Korea bought more from the United States and Taiwan and made more at home.

   + g-line, i-line steppers, i-line scanners
   + i-line is the coarsest of the main lithography lights, 365 nanometers from a mercury lamp, named for one line in the lamp's spectrum. It prints the widest features on a chip and much of a package.
   + SMEE, the established Chinese scanner maker, is strongest in i-line, and even there it holds about 4 percent of the world market.

   + Important · Equipment subsystems · Gas and fluid delivery subsystems

   + integrated device manufacturer, integrated device maker, integrated device makers
   + A chip company that both designs its products and manufactures them in its own factories. Intel, Samsung and Micron are examples.
   + An integrated maker that also makes chips for other companies competes with its own customers. Its own products take the best capacity, so fewer customers trust it with a flagship design.

   + immersion DUV, ArF immersion, immersion scanner, immersion scanners, immersion tools, immersion DUV scanners, immersion deep-ultraviolet
   + Immersion lithography prints with a thin layer of ultrapure water between the scanner's lens and the wafer. The water lets the lens focus light at a wider angle than air allows, so the same light prints finer lines. It is the most advanced form of deep ultraviolet lithography, one step below extreme ultraviolet.
   + Dutch export licensing covers the most advanced immersion scanners. Reuters reported in July 2026, citing one unnamed source, that a Chinese state-owned firm had started producing its own immersion scanners.
   + Read it in Lithography Also in Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist, Transistors and the Front End, Who Controls the Chain

   + Critical · Photomasks · Multi-beam mask writers
   + Builds the multi-beam electron mask writers that pattern EUV masks with hundreds of thousands of parallel beamlets. On CSET's count it led the market for all electron-beam mask writers in 2025. NuFlare is the only other maker of multi-beam writers.
   + transfer (in-country)
   + A change in the end use or the end user of an item inside one foreign country, defined at 15 CFR 734.16. Nothing crosses a border. A server legally imported into a country for one customer is transferred when it is resold, or repurposed, for another.
   + Controls on in-country transfers catch hardware diverted after it has arrived. So a license granted for a named Chinese customer does not cover the hardware if it passes to someone else. Reexports and in-country transfers of advanced chips to Macau or Country Group D:5 keep a presumption of denial, meaning a license will normally be refused (91 FR 1684). That holds even where an export from the United States would be reviewed case by case (91 FR 1684).
   + InP, indium
   + Indium phosphide is a crystal grown from the metal indium and phosphorus. It gives off light when current passes through it, which silicon cannot do efficiently. The lasers inside the fastest optical modules are made of it.
   + China produced about 70 percent of the world's indium in 2025. In February 2025 it put indium under new export restrictions and began requiring export permits for indium phosphide substrates. In March 2026 Nvidia agreed to invest $2 billion each in Coherent and Lumentum, alongside purchase commitments for their lasers.
   + inference chip, inference chips, inference accelerator, inference accelerators, training and inference
   + Inference is running a finished AI model to answer requests, such as a prompt. Training, a run of weeks on a cluster of accelerators, builds the model first. To write each word of an answer, the chip reads the whole model from memory, does a little arithmetic and waits for the next read. Training reuses each number it fetches, so it fetches little and computes a lot.
   + In inference, memory size and speed usually set how many people one chip can serve.
   + Read it in Memory and HBM Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Who Controls the Chain

   + Supplies the MOSFETs, gallium nitride devices and power stages used from the grid connection through the rack power shelf to the point-of-load regulator.    + Quantum-2, Quantum-X800
   + InfiniBand is a networking standard long used in supercomputers, with low delay and no dropped packets. AI clusters relied on it to link thousands of servers before Ethernet caught up, and Nvidia still sells both.
   + The choice between InfiniBand and Ethernet decides who sells the switch chips. Broadcom has doubled the bandwidth of its Ethernet switch chips with each generation since 2019.
   + Important · Optics & photonics · 800G and 1.6T optical transceivers
   + A world-leading maker of high-speed optical transceiver modules for data centers, shipping 800G and 1.6T modules to hyperscalers. AI clusters need one or more optical modules per accelerator.
   + Important · Photoresist · Metal-oxide EUV photoresist
   + Developed tin-oxide EUV resists that absorb EUV photons far more efficiently than organic chemically amplified resists. Higher absorption cuts the light dose needed and sharpens resolution on High-NA EUV tools.
   + Important · Accelerator vendors · Server CPUs and AI accelerators
   + Designs Xeon server CPUs and the Gaudi accelerator line. Xeon chips are still the host CPUs in most AI clusters. Its product business is separate from Intel Foundry, the company's manufacturing arm.

   + interposers, silicon interposer, silicon carrier
   + A layer of wiring with no transistors, used as a miniature circuit board underneath several chips so they can be connected at a density no ordinary board could reach. It is either one piece of silicon or layers of fine copper wiring, often with small silicon bridges.
   + HBM connects over 1,024 or 2,048 wires, too many for a resin board, so memory and logic sit together on an interposer. Its area caps how much fits in one package. In April 2026 TSMC was producing CoWoS at 5.5 times the reticle, the largest area a lithography machine prints in one exposure.
   + Read it in Advanced Packaging Also in Memory and HBM, Substrates and PCBs
   + Important · Systems & servers · AI server ODM
   + Important · Systems & servers · AI server ODM
   + Builds AI and general purpose servers for cloud operators and OEMs, with manufacturing in Taiwan, Mexico, the Czech Republic and Malaysia.
   + ion implanter, ion implanters, implant, implant layers, implantation
   + A way of changing how silicon conducts by firing atoms into it. An ion implanter shoots charged atoms of boron, phosphorus or arsenic into the wafer, where they lodge just under the surface. The patches they land in then conduct the way the design needs.
   + Every modern chip process relies on it. Implanters are export-controlled. In February 2026 Applied Materials agreed to pay a penalty of about $252 million over implanters shipped to a Chinese customer on the Entity List.
   + silicon IP, IP blocks, licensed blocks, licensed circuit blocks, semiconductor IP, IP catalog, intellectual property
   + In chip design, IP, short for intellectual property, means a ready-made block of circuitry, such as a processor core or a memory interface, that a design house licenses. Arm sells processor IP, and other firms sell the blocks that talk to memory, PCIe or another chiplet.
   + Each licensed block must be proven on a new process before a chip can use it. The memory interface is a demanding one, because it has to hold its timing across thousands of solder bumps into a stacked memory die.
   + is informed letter, is-informed letters, by letter
   + A letter from BIS to one named company imposing a license requirement on a specific export, without any rule being published. BIS's power to send one is written into the regulations themselves, for example 15 CFR 744.23(b), which lets BIS inform a person individually, with no amendment to the EAR.
   + It imposes a control at once, with no published rule. BIS imposed the April 2025 license requirement for Nvidia's H20 this way, and the public learned of it from Nvidia's own filing.

   + Contested · Advanced packaging · OSAT assembly, test and fan-out packaging

   + JESD270-4, JEDEC standard
   + JEDEC is the industry body that writes the standards for memory chips, so that a memory stack from any maker fits any processor built to the same specification. Its HBM4 standard, published in April 2025, sets the width and speed of the interface.
   + Makers now ship HBM4 faster than the standard's 8 Gb/s per pin. Samsung's runs at 11.7, SK hynix's above 10 and Micron's above 11. Each customer runs its own qualification tests, so meeting the standard does not by itself win an order.

   + kW, kilowatts, megawatt, MW, megawatts, gigawatt, GW, gigawatts
   + A kilowatt is a thousand watts. A megawatt is a thousand kilowatts, and a gigawatt a thousand megawatts, about the output of one large power station. A GB300 NVL72 rack draws up to 142 kilowatts. The largest data center campuses are planned in gigawatts.
   + AI deals are now counted in gigawatts too, as when OpenAI and Broadcom announced 10 gigawatts of accelerators. Power equipment now arrives more slowly than the chips.

   + KGD, good die, good dies, known good die
   + A bare chip tested thoroughly enough, before assembly, to be trusted inside something expensive. In an ordinary product a faulty chip wastes one chip. Inside a package that joins a dozen expensive pieces, one bad die throws away all of them.
   + As packages grow toward about ten large compute dies and 20 memory stacks, a bad die found only after assembly scraps more parts. So chipmakers move testing and burn-in, running chips hot to force early failures, back onto the wafer.
   + Read it in Test and Assembly Also in Metrology and Inspection
   + Important · Deposition & etch · Batch ALD and vertical furnaces
   + Makes batch vertical furnaces and batch ALD systems that process dozens of wafers at once. Memory makers use them widely for DRAM and 3D NAND film steps where throughput matters more than single-wafer control.
   + krypton fluoride, krypton fluoride laser, 248 nm, 248-nanometer
   + KrF is krypton fluoride, the gas in an older lithography laser that shines at 248 nanometers. KrF scanners print the coarser layers of a chip, where lines are far apart.
   + KrF tools print coarser layers such as implant layers. ASML sold 78 of them in 2025, and Canon also makes them.
   + Important · Advanced packaging · Wire bonders and advanced assembly
   + Makes wire bonders, the standard tool of low-cost packaging, and is developing thermocompression and fluxless bonding systems for advanced packages.

   + Important · Deposition & etch · Plasma etch, deposition and wafer clean
   + The largest supplier of plasma etch systems. According to CSET, it had 41.1 percent of the $21.9 billion dry etch tool market in 2025, against 23.4 percent for Tokyo Electron. Its line includes the high aspect ratio etch used to drill 3D NAND channels and DRAM capacitors, plus atomic layer deposition, electroplating and cryogenic etch tools.
   + laminates, copper-clad laminate, copper-clad laminates, low-loss laminate, core laminate
   + A laminate is a stiff sheet made by pressing glass cloth and resin together, usually with copper foil bonded to its faces. It is the material a circuit board or a substrate core is cut from. A low-loss laminate absorbs little of the signal passing through the wires on it.
   + How much signal the laminate absorbs has become a main design limit on the boards that link GPUs.
   + large power transformers, transformer, transformers, switchgear, substation
   + A large power transformer is a machine that changes grid electricity from one voltage to another, for example from transmission voltage down to a level a data center can use. Switchgear is the heavy switching and protection equipment around it. Together they make up the substation at the gate of the campus.
   + In 2024 lead times of 36 months were commonly quoted, against under a year before the pandemic. Of the units put into US service in 2019, 82 percent were imported.
   + Critical · Metrology · EUV mask blank and pattern inspection
   + Makes actinic inspection tools, which use EUV light to inspect EUV mask blanks and patterned masks. Its ACTIS A150 was the world's first actinic EUV patterned mask inspection system. In October 2025 it launched the ACTIS A200HiT series, which it says triples the A150's inspection speed. These tools detect printable phase defects, flaws in a mask's reflective layers that DUV-based mask inspection cannot find.
   + lead times, delivery time
   + Lead time is the wait between placing an order and receiving it. A wafer clears a leading-edge fab in months, while in 2024 a large power transformer commonly took three years.
   + Chip supply and power equipment are both constrained. Micron said in its report for the quarter to May 2026 that AI-driven memory and storage growth was outpacing industry supply. Waits for large power equipment run to years.
   + leading-edge, at the leading edge, advanced node, advanced nodes, advanced-node
   + The leading edge is the newest generation of chipmaking process in volume production. In 2026 that means the 3 nm and 2 nm nodes, made only by TSMC, Samsung and Intel. A leading-edge chip is one built on it.
   + Gate-all-around transistors are the design used at 2 nm, and only TSMC, Intel and Samsung make them in volume. In 2024 the United States put a worldwide license requirement on the technology for building them.
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Silicon and Wafers, Lithography, Photomasks and Pellicles, Foundries and Fabs, Memory and HBM, Test and Assembly, Data Centers and Power, The Economics of AI Chips, Who Controls the Chain
   + Where it appears, 61 places
   + Diversified · Systems & servers · AI servers and liquid cooling
   + Builds AI servers and its Neptune warm-water liquid cooling systems, selling into enterprises and research computing worldwide from plants in China, Hungary, Mexico and the US.
   + Important · Substrates & PCBs · FC-BGA substrates and camera modules
   + Entered high-end flip-chip BGA substrates in 2022 with a plant in Gumi, adding a third Korean source alongside Samsung Electro-Mechanics. Its larger business is optical modules for smartphones.
   + license exception
   + A written carve-out in export rules that lets a shipment go without an individual license, if it meets stated conditions. The ones that matter for AI chips and chipmaking tools are NAC and ACA (15 CFR 740.8), HBM (740.25), AIA and LPP (740.27 and 740.29), IEC (740.24) and RPL (740.10). AIA and LPP let the top band of accelerators ship to allies, and elsewhere in small volumes. IEC covers countries that adopt matching controls, and RPL covers servicing and spare parts.
   + Exceptions let the US government adjust its controls without rewriting them and ease the rules for allies. License Exception IEC, added in September 2024, covers exports to and by countries that have put equivalent controls in place (89 FR 72926).
   + ACA, Advanced Computing Authorized
   + An export rule that lets AI chips other than top-band data center chips ship to most of the world with no license and no advance notice. It covers the same chips as its companion exception NAC, and every destination except Macau, Country Group D:5 and firms headquartered in either. The top-band data center chips in ECCN 3A090.a are excluded. BIS split it from NAC at 15 CFR 740.8 in April 2024. The AI Diffusion rule widened it in January 2025 to the whole world outside Macau and D:5.
   + It lets these chips reach most of the world with no license or advance notice. NAC keeps a notice requirement for Macau and D:5. Before April 2024 NAC did both jobs, so an older table that shows one exception describes what is now two.
   + ACM, Advanced Compute Manufacturing
   + License Exception ACM, for Advanced Compute Manufacturing, lets controlled AI chips and related items go without a license to private-sector firms that develop, make or store them. The items must be bound for customers outside Macau and Country Group D:5. It was created alongside License Exceptions AIA and LPP.
   + It keeps the AI Diffusion rule's worldwide license requirement from catching chips that are only passing through factories and warehouses on their way to an eligible customer.
   + AIA, Artificial Intelligence Authorization
   + License Exception AIA, for Artificial Intelligence Authorization, lets the top band of AI chips ship without a license to nineteen destinations. They are listed in supplement no. 5 to part 740, and the United States is among them. The AI Diffusion rule created it in January 2025 as the exception inside a worldwide license requirement.
   + BIS said in May 2025 that it would not enforce the AI Diffusion rule. But the rule was never repealed, so AIA still stands in the regulations. BIS says it enforces the worldwide requirement only for Country Groups D:1, D:4 and D:5 and for firms headquartered in D:5 or Macau.
   + An export rule that lets high bandwidth memory go to a packaging site with no license, to be packaged beside a chip. It applies if the memory's bandwidth density is below 3.3 GB/s per square millimeter, measured over the area of the package or stack. The exporter must be headquartered in the United States or Country Group A:5. If the memory is bound for Macau or Country Group D:5, the chip's designer must buy it. The stacks must go straight to the packaging site. It does not cover packaging at a facility in Macau or D:5 that also makes advanced-node chips. It sits at 15 CFR 740.25.
   + It lets Chinese packaging houses stay in the supply chain of American and allied chip designers. But a sale of the same stacks to a Chinese buyer still needs a license. HBM needs a license only for Macau and Country Group D:5, and the exception was written into the December 2024 rule that first controlled HBM (89 FR 96790).
   + LPP, Low Processing Performance
   + An export rule that lets a buyer almost anywhere receive a capped amount of top-band AI chips each year without a license. The cap is 26,900,000 TPP, the export rules' measure of computing power, per ultimate consignee per calendar year. Macau, Country Group D:5 and firms headquartered in either are excluded, and it does not cover transfers within a country. The AI Diffusion rule created it in January 2025, at 15 CFR 740.29.
   + It lets buyers outside the nineteen destinations in supplement no. 5 to part 740 take top AI chips in small amounts with no license application. BIS said in May 2025 that it would not enforce the AI Diffusion rule, but it withdrew none of the text. So LPP still stands in the regulations beside the rule's other exceptions, AIA and ACM, and the nineteen-destination list.
   + NAC, Notified Advanced Computing
   + An export rule that lets AI chips other than top-band data center chips ship to Macau and Country Group D:5, and to firms headquartered there, with no license. The exporter must notify BIS at least 25 calendar days ahead, and the shipment can go if BIS does not say a license is needed. The top-band data center chips in ECCN 3A090.a and the memory in 3A090.c are excluded. It sits at 15 CFR 740.8.
   + It lets these chips reach the restricted destinations without a license application. Since April 2024 section 740.8 has held two exceptions, NAC for the restricted destinations and ACA for everywhere else.
   + Contested · Optics & photonics · Photonic interconnect and compute
   + Develops photonic interposers and optical interconnect that route light beneath stacked dies, aiming to remove the bandwidth limit between accelerators in a package.
   + Important · Chemicals & gases · Bulk and electronic specialty gases
   + The world's largest industrial gas company. It supplies fabs with nitrogen, hydrogen, helium and rare gases such as the neon used in excimer lasers, usually from plants built next to the fab. Gas supply contracts run for a decade or more.
   + direct-to-chip cooling, cold plate cooling, cold plate, cold plates, liquid-cooled, direct-to-chip liquid, direct liquid cooling
   + Taking heat out of a processor by pumping liquid through a cold plate on the chip package, instead of blowing air across a finned heatsink. A building loop then carries the heat outdoors.
   + Nvidia's GB300 NVL72 rack is liquid cooled, and its Rubin platform is entirely liquid cooled. Nvidia says every cloud provider and data center operator building for Rubin is moving to liquid cooling.
   + photolithography
   + Printing a circuit pattern onto a wafer by shining light through a patterned plate and projecting a shrunken image of it onto a light-sensitive coating. It works like photography at the scale of nanometers, repeated once for every layer of the chip.
   + Only one company, ASML, makes the machines for its finest layers, and those layers set how densely transistors and wires pack.
   + Read it in Lithography Also in Silicon and Wafers, Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist, Photomasks and Pellicles, Deposition and Etch, Transistors and the Front End, Advanced Packaging, Substrates and PCBs, Test and Assembly, Who Controls the Chain
   + logic chips, logic die, logic dies
   + A logic chip is one that computes, such as a processor, a graphics processor or an AI accelerator. Memory chips, such as DRAM and NAND flash, store data instead.
   + US export rules set separate lines for logic and memory. The October 2022 rule covered equipment for fabs in China making logic chips at 16/14 nm or below. It also covered fabs making DRAM at 18 nm half-pitch or below, or NAND with 128 or more layers. ASML's 2025 system sales were €16,054.1 million to logic chipmakers and €8,420.2 million to memory makers.
   + Read it in Foundries and Fabs Also in Silicon and Wafers, Deposition and Etch, Metrology and Inspection, Transistors and the Front End, Advanced Packaging, Substrates and PCBs, Test and Assembly, The Economics of AI Chips, Who Controls the Chain
   + Where it appears, 32 places

   + lots, wafer lot, wafer lots, per lot
   + A lot is the batch of wafers a fab moves through its machines together, usually 25 in one sealed carrier. Recipes, inspections and yield are tracked lot by lot.
   + A fab decides how many wafers in each lot it can afford to inspect. That choice sets how much it spends on process control, the measuring that keeps each step on target.
   + Read it in Metrology and Inspection Also in Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist, Memory and HBM

   + Macao, China or Macau, China and Macau
   + Macau is a Chinese city with its own customs territory, like Hong Kong, so goods can enter it under separate rules. US export controls name China and Macau together to close that route, and treat a firm headquartered in either the same way.
   + Under the US rules on advanced AI chips, a chip that needs a license for China needs one for Macau too.
