   + Important · Accelerator vendors · Custom AI silicon, optical DSPs and interconnect
   + Designs custom AI accelerators and infrastructure chips for hyperscalers, plus networking chips such as optical DSPs, retimers and Ethernet controllers for AI clusters. It ranks second to Broadcom in custom AI chips and competes with it directly to design hyperscalers' accelerators.
   + mask blanks, EUV blank, EUV blanks, photomask blank, photomask blanks, blank plate, DUV blanks
   + A mask blank is a photomask before any pattern is on it. It is a six-inch square of very flat glass carrying the film that the pattern will be cut into. For extreme ultraviolet that film sits on a mirror of about forty pairs of ultrathin layers, and a particle buried in the mirror cannot be repaired.
   + The Japanese glassmaker AGC says only three firms supply EUV blanks, and AGC and Hoya, both Japanese, are two of them. AGC spent more than a decade meeting the blank's requirements before it began mass production in 2017.
   + Read it in Photomasks and Pellicles Also in Silicon and Wafers
   + mask shops, mask making, mask maker, mask makers
   + A mask shop is the factory that turns a finished chip design into its set of photomasks. It writes each plate with an electron beam, inspects it and repairs it.
   + No chip can be printed until its masks are made. Chipmakers such as TSMC and SMIC run shops of their own, and independents such as Photronics sell to them and to others.
   + Read it in Photomasks and Pellicles Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Lithography
   + mask writers, multi-beam writer, multi-beam writers, electron-beam writer, e-beam writer, mask writing
   + A mask writer is the machine that uses electrons to draw a chip's pattern into the resist on a photomask blank. A single-beam writer flashes one shape at a time, while NuFlare's multi-beam writer for the most advanced masks controls 260,000 beams at once.
   + No new chip can be printed until its masks are written. In 2023 Intel said advanced EUV requires multi-beam mask writers and called Austria's IMS Nanofabrication the established leader in them. Counting all electron-beam mask writers, CSET put IMS at 46.6 percent of 2025 sales and Japan's NuFlare at 39.2 percent.
   + Read it in Photomasks and Pellicles Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP
   + mature nodes, mature-node, mature logic, older nodes, older lines, trailing edge
   + A mature node is an older chipmaking process, 28 nm and above, that has run for years and competes on price. Most of the world's wafers come off these lines, making power chips, analog parts and microcontrollers.
   + China held 33 percent of the world's logic capacity at 28 nm and above in 2022. In the five years before May 2024, Chinese companies invested $63 billion in 73 fabs, mostly for chips above 28 nm, SIA and BCG reported.
   + Read it in Foundries and Fabs Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Silicon and Wafers, Who Controls the Chain
   + Important · Accelerator vendors · Custom ASIC and mobile SoCs
   + Best known for its smartphone chips, MediaTek also designs custom AI chips (ASICs) and works on Google's TPU program. It has experience designing high-volume chips on advanced nodes.
   + bandwidth density, bandwidth-density
   + Memory bandwidth density is how much data a memory stack can move each second divided by the area of its package or stack, in gigabytes per second per square millimeter. The December 2024 rule controls any stack above 2, and BIS said every HBM stack then in production exceeded that.
   + One threshold catches every HBM stack in production, including stacks made outside the United States, so the control reaches all three makers, SK hynix, Samsung and Micron. A license exception still lets stacks below 3.3 go straight to a chip designer's packaging site. HBM already packaged with a processor falls under the chip rules instead.

   + The widening gap between how fast a processor can calculate and how fast memory can feed it numbers to work on. The cost of arithmetic has fallen faster than the cost of moving numbers.
   + An AI chip reads its whole model from memory for each word it writes, so memory size and speed usually set how many people one chip can serve. Memory is also about half the cost of building an Nvidia B200, on Epoch AI's estimate.

   + metal-oxide resists, metal oxide resist, tin-oxide resist, metal-oxide EUV photoresist
   + A metal-oxide resist is a photoresist built on clusters with a tin-oxide core. It absorbs more extreme ultraviolet light than a chemically amplified resist. That helps because an EUV dose arrives as relatively few photons.
   + Resist makers aim it at High-NA tools and the tightest pitches. The Japanese resist maker JSR agreed in 2021 to buy Inpria, an Oregon maker of these resists. In 2024 a company owned by a Japanese state-backed fund took 84.36 percent of JSR.

   + METI is Japan's Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry, the ministry that writes Japan's export controls and funds its chip industry. It put advanced chipmaking equipment under export license from July 2023. It also supports the chipmaker Rapidus and new capacity for mask blanks, the glass plates that photomasks are made from.

   + process control, when grouped with inspection, metrology and inspection, inspection tools
   + Measuring what a factory actually built: how wide a printed line came out, how thick a film is, whether this layer sits exactly on top of the last one. Inspection is the related job of finding flaws.
   + A fab that cannot buy sensitive inspection tools finds fewer of the defects that lower its yield, so its yield rises more slowly. KLA held 56.8 percent of the market for these tools in 2025, by CSET's count.
   + Read it in Metrology and Inspection Also in Who Controls the Chain
   + Where it appears, 48 places
   + microbumps, solder bump, solder bumps, bumps, bump, bump metrology
   + A microbump is a dot of solder that joins a die to the interposer or substrate beneath it. In Intel's 2.5D packages microbumps sit 36 microns apart, less than half a hair's width. Each one is an electrical connection that has to survive as the package heats and cools.
   + Their spacing limits how many connections fit between stacked or neighboring chips. Hybrid bonding, which joins copper straight to copper, would pack connections far closer, but it is not yet ready for HBM.

   + square millimeters, square millimeter, mm²
   + mm2 means square millimeters, the unit chip areas are given in. A lithography scanner prints at most 858 mm2 in one shot, and each compute die in an Nvidia B200 is about 800 mm2. TSMC plans to start making CoWoS packages with interposers 14 times the size of one scanner shot in 2028.
   + Yield, the share of dies that work, falls as die area grows, so the cost of each working chip rises faster than its area.
   + Read it in The Economics of AI Chips Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Transistors and the Front End, Memory and HBM, Advanced Packaging
   + weights, frontier model weights, trained weights
   + Model weights are the billions of numbers that make up a trained AI model. They are the file that runs on the chips, so copying the weights copies the model.
   + A chip reads the model's weights from memory for each word it writes, so bigger models need more HBM. The AI Diffusion rule also made frontier model weights a controlled item, ECCN 4E091.
   + Ministry of Commerce, China's Ministry of Commerce

   + China's retaliation targets inputs such as these minerals, and MOFCOM signs each notice.

   + double patterning, quadruple patterning, self-aligned double patterning, self-aligned quadruple patterning, SADP, SAQP, LELE, multiple patterning, multi-patterning, several passes, several exposures, multiply exposed, DUV multipatterning
   + Building one chip layer in several patterning steps, because a single exposure cannot print lines that close together. It comes in two kinds. Litho-etch multipatterning splits the layer's pattern across two or three masks, then prints and etches each part separately. Self-aligned double or quadruple patterning prints one coarser pattern, then deposits and etches thin spacer films along its edges to halve or quarter the line spacing. Cut masks then remove the parts of lines the circuit does not need.
   + SMIC makes its 7 nm-class chips without extreme ultraviolet machines by multipatterning on deep ultraviolet tools. TSMC's first 7 nm process and Intel 7 were also made without extreme ultraviolet light. Every extra mask or spacer step adds cost and machine time, and each extra litho-etch exposure adds a chance that parts of the layer misalign.

   + Important · Substrates & PCBs · ABF substrates and printed circuit boards
   + Produces ABF and BT IC substrates and high-layer-count printed circuit boards, and is one of the main IC substrate makers worldwide.
   + 3D NAND, NAND flash, flash memory, 128-layer NAND
   + NAND is the memory that keeps data when the power is off. It is the storage inside a phone, a laptop drive or a data center disk. 3D NAND stacks its cells in many layers, made by etching holes far deeper than they are wide. It is slow next to DRAM and is not used inside an AI accelerator.
   + Under the October 2022 US rules, items subject to US export rules bound for a fab in China that makes NAND with 128 or more layers need a license. The proposed MATCH Act would name YMTC, a Chinese NAND maker, in the law itself.
   + nm, nanometers, microns, micrometer, micrometers, angstrom, angstroms, picometer, picometers
   + A nanometer is a billionth of a meter. A micron, or micrometer, is a thousand nanometers, an angstrom is a tenth of a nanometer, and a picometer is a thousandth. Lithography light is measured in nanometers, package bumps in microns, and mirror flatness in picometers.
   + Node names such as 2 nm no longer describe any feature that size, but the physical limits of printing and stacking are still measured in these units.

   + neon gas, semiconductor-grade neon
   + Neon, mixed with krypton or argon and fluorine, is the gas inside the lasers that give deep ultraviolet scanners their light. A fab uses it by the cylinder, and those scanners cannot print without it.
   + Ukraine supplied about 70 percent of the world's neon before Russia invaded in 2022. Many chipmakers had added to their gas stockpiles after Russia annexed Crimea in 2014, and several reported adequate supplies from China.

   + NF3
   + Nitrogen trifluoride is a gas that cleans process chambers between wafers. Split apart in a plasma, it releases fluorine that scours the deposits off the inside of the chamber.
   + A shortage of any one chemical can stop a fab. SK Specialty of South Korea has made nitrogen trifluoride since 2001, when it became the first company to produce the gas there.

It says Chinese and Taiwanese companies are aggressively catching up with it in special glass.
   + 5 nm-class, 7 nm-class, 2 nm-class, nanometer-class, nm class
   + Nm-class, as in 7 nm-class, means a process roughly as dense as the foundries' 7 nm generation. The label is used when a maker's own name for the node is different.
   + China's SMIC builds its 7 nm-class processes without extreme ultraviolet machines, and its most advanced, N+3, reaches the density of TSMC's 6 nm-class process.
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End Also in Foundries and Fabs, Who Controls the Chain
   + process node, technology node, for example N2 or 18A, nodes
   + The name a foundry gives one generation of its manufacturing process. The number in the name no longer measures anything on the chip. SMIC's N+3, a 7 nm-class process, spaces its narrowest wires closer together than Intel's Panther Lake chips on 18A do.
   + Comparing two foundries by node name says little. Each foundry also picks its own baseline for the density gains it claims.

   + GB200 NVL72, GB300 NVL72, Vera Rubin NVL72
   + Nvidia's rack-sized product, with 72 accelerators in 18 compute trays joined through nine switch trays so that software can treat the whole rack as one computer. The GB300 version draws up to 142 kilowatts.
   + AI capacity is built and costed by the rack. In July 2026 SemiAnalysis put the cost of owning a Vera Rubin NVL72 at $3.57 per GPU-hour, against $1.84 for a GB200 and $2.36 for a GB300.

   + NVSwitch, NVLink 5, NVLink 6, NV-HBI
   + Nvidia's own high-speed connection between its accelerators, which lets dozens of them act as one. NVLink 6 carries 3,600 gigabytes per second per GPU, according to Nvidia's January 2026 Rubin launch.
   + Nvidia controls it, while more than 50 makers of systems and components build around its racks. Since May 2025 its NVLink Fusion program has let other firms' custom chips connect to Nvidia GPUs over NVLink, and Fujitsu and Qualcomm plan CPUs that do so. UALink is a published open rival.

   + Diversified · Silicon wafers · Polysilicon and electronic-grade chemicals
   + A Korean chemical group that makes polysilicon and fab chemicals, including high-purity hydrogen peroxide and phosphoric acid. Most of its polysilicon goes to solar customers.
   + original design manufacturer, ODMs, contract manufacturer, contract manufacturers, contract manufacturing, EMS, server ODM
   + An ODM, for original design manufacturer, is a firm such as Foxconn, Quanta or Wistron that designs and builds servers and racks to a customer's specification. The customer's name goes on the box. A contract manufacturer does the assembly only.
   + More than 50 makers of systems and components were gearing up for Nvidia's Vera Rubin NVL72 rack in October 2025, Nvidia said. In its filing for the quarter to July 2026, Nvidia said the complexity of producing its data center systems had caused production delays.
   + oligopolies, duopoly, three-firm oligopoly
   + An oligopoly is a market with only a few sellers, so each one's decisions move the price. A duopoly has two. DRAM, industrial gases, substrates and chip testers are all oligopolies.
   + The fewer the sellers, the more one accident or one license decision moves the market. With a single seller, losing that seller stops the buyer's production line.
   + Important · Metrology · Optical metrology, inspection and lithography software
   + Supplies optical film metrology, macro defect inspection and packaging inspection, with a strong position in advanced packaging metrology. It was formed from Rudolph Technologies and Nanometrics.
   + Announced 2026-06 · TSMC N3P compute die, N3E I/O chiplet (per SemiAnalysis) · HBM4 (per SemiAnalysis) · 700 W
   + Engineering samples. OpenAI plans to begin deploying it by the end of 2026
   + operating profit, operating loss, operating income
   + Operating margin is the share of revenue left after paying both the cost of making the product and the cost of running the company, research and sales included. It is lower than gross margin, which counts only the cost of making.
   + SK hynix's earnings release gives no gross margin, only an operating margin, which was 76 percent in the June 2026 quarter.
   + Read it in The Economics of AI Chips Also in Silicon and Wafers, Lithography, Foundries and Fabs
   + optical module, pluggable optics, optical transceivers, transceiver, transceivers, optical modules, 800G optical transceivers
   + A small device that turns electrical signals into laser light for sending down a glass fiber, and turns them back at the far end. Every network link longer than a few meters in a large AI cluster needs one at each end.
   + A cluster needs several for each accelerator. One design for 100,000 H100s uses 98,304 just to link its GPUs to the first tier of switches. Chinese firms assemble the modules in volume, but the lasers inside them were scarce in 2026.
   + Read it in Systems and Networking Also in The Economics of AI Chips
   + outsourced semiconductor assembly and test, OSATs, outsourced assembly and test, assembly and test firms, assembly and test contractor, assembly and test houses
   + A contract firm that takes finished wafers, cuts them into chips, seals the chips into packages and tests them. It is the back end of chipmaking, done for hire.
   + Every chip passes through assembly and test. Yet in the June 2026 quarter Amkor earned a gross margin of 16.8 percent, and ASE's packaging and test segment 27.3 percent. In a TrendForce ranking cited by CSET, four of the ten largest of these firms by 2024 revenue are headquartered in China.
   + overlay error, alignment
   + How precisely one printed layer of a chip lands on top of the layer beneath it. Before each exposure the scanner finds marks printed in earlier layers and lines the new layer up on them to within a few nanometers, across a disc 300 millimeters wide.
   + Misalignment can ruin whole regions of a wafer, so overlay is one of the specifications that separates an advanced printing machine from an ordinary one. Each extra pass on a layer is another chance to misalign.
   + Read it in Metrology and Inspection Also in Lithography

   + Important · Substrates & PCBs · Copper-clad laminates and electronic materials
   + Makes the Megtron series of low-loss copper-clad laminates used in high-speed server and switch boards. At 112 Gbps, too much signal loss in the board makes a link fail.
   + panel-level, panel level packaging, rectangular panel, rectangular panels
   + Panel-level packaging builds packages on a large rectangular sheet instead of a round wafer, so far less area is wasted at the edges and many more packages fit on one carrier. It gets around the size limits of a 300 mm wafer.
   + Powertech, a Taiwanese assembly and test firm, aims to run the first panel-level packaging line for AI chips in 2027.
   + Read it in Advanced Packaging Also in Test and Assembly
   + Read it in Advanced Packaging Also in Test and Assembly
   + Important · Metrology · Atomic force microscopy
   + Makes the automated atomic force microscopes fabs use to measure surface roughness, step height and defect profiles at sub-nanometer resolution. It acquired the ellipsometry maker Accurion in 2022 and the holographic microscopy maker Lyncée Tec in 2025.
   + PCI Express, PCIe 6, PCIe 7
   + PCIe is the standard connector and link that joins a processor to the cards inside a computer, an accelerator included. In an AI server it carries data between the host processor and the GPUs, while faster private links join the GPUs to each other.
   + The PCIe block is licensed IP that has to be proven on each new node before a chip can use it.
   + process design kit, process design kits
   + A process design kit, or PDK, is the set of files a foundry gives designers to describe exactly what its factory can print on one process, or node. It gives the sizes, spacings and electrical behavior of every feature. Design software is checked against it before a chip is accepted.
   + Foundries develop each kit with the design-software vendors about two years before production starts and specify which tools customers must use for tape-out.
   + pellicles, EUV pellicle
   + A thin transparent membrane on a frame above a photomask, the patterned plate a chip layer is printed from. Dust settles on it, far enough out of focus that it does not print, so the pattern stays clean. It works like a lens cap a photographer can shoot through.
   + Pellicles already work in high-volume EUV manufacturing. But no material is completely transparent to EUV light, and the light crosses the membrane twice, so every pellicle costs some output. Carbon nanotube membranes, which passed up to 97 percent of the light in one crossing in imec's 2020 tests, are the next step.
   + Read it in Photomasks and Pellicles
   + An export control test of how densely a chip packs its computing power. It divides the chip's TPP, the export rules' measure of computing power, by its applicable die area in square millimeters. That area counts all logic dies made on a process with non-planar transistors, such as FinFETs. The test applies only to a chip that reaches 1,600 TPP. It replaced the 2022 test, which was based on interconnect bandwidth, the speed at which a chip exchanges data with its neighbors.
   + It closed a gap that Nvidia's China-specific chips, such as the A800 and H800, had used. Nvidia had designed those chips to stay under the 2022 interconnect-speed threshold. The top band, ECCN 3A090.a, now starts at 4,800 TPP, or at 1,600 TPP with a performance density of 5.92 (88 FR 73458).
   + Important · Equipment subsystems · Turbomolecular pumps and leak detection
   + Makes turbomolecular pumps, which spin at tens of thousands of revolutions per minute to reach the high vacuum that etch, deposition and EUV chambers require. It also makes leak detection instruments.
   + photomask
   + mask, reticle, mask set, photomasks, masks, mask sets, mask blank set
   + The master template for one layer of a chip: a quartz plate, or for the shortest wavelengths a mirror, that carries the pattern projected onto the wafer. A chip needs dozens of them, at least one per layer. In July 2022 SemiAnalysis put a full set above $1 million at 28 nm and above $10 million at 7 nm. In May 2026 it put a leading-edge set at $40 million.
   + The cost of a mask set limits which chips are worth making on a leading-edge process at all. Only three firms supply the blank plates for EUV masks, and two of them are Japanese.
   + Read it in Photomasks and Pellicles Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Silicon and Wafers, Lithography
   + resist, resists, EUV resist, EUV resists, resist makers
   + The light-sensitive coating that records a chip's pattern during lithography. A machine spins a thin film of it across the wafer. Light shone through a stencil changes the film where it lands, and a wash removes those parts. What remains shows the next machine where to etch.
   + Every printed layer of a chip uses it. Each resist is tuned to the layers it prints, and even one that passes the first time takes seven to 17 months to approve in a fab. Japanese companies held 78 percent of world sales in 2023. In 2024 an investment vehicle of a Japanese state-backed fund bought 84.36 percent of the resist maker JSR.
   + Read it in Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist Also in Silicon and Wafers, Lithography, Deposition and Etch
   + Where it appears, 31 places
   + Important · Photomasks · Merchant photomask manufacturing
   + A merchant photomask maker, writing and inspecting masks for logic and display customers from plants in the US, Taiwan, Korea, China and Europe. The foundries make most leading-edge masks in-house.
   + Contested · Deposition & etch · PECVD, ALD and wafer bonding
   + China's main domestic supplier of plasma-enhanced CVD and ALD systems, and more recently hybrid wafer bonding tools aimed at 3D integration.
   + pitches, half-pitch, gate pitch, metal pitch, wire pitch, line pitch
   + Pitch is the distance from one line on a chip to the next, center to center, so it counts the line and the gap together. Half-pitch is half that. A 16 nm pitch means lines 16 nanometers apart. The 2022 US rules set a DRAM threshold at 18 nm half-pitch.
   + Unlike a node name, pitch measures something physical on the chip. But SemiAnalysis calls a single pitch an incomplete metric for comparing processes.
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End Also in Deposition and Etch, Metrology and Inspection
   + plasmas, plasma etch, plasma-enhanced, plasma source
   + A plasma is a gas energized until some of its atoms or molecules break into charged particles. Most etch steps and many deposition steps happen in a plasma that an electric field makes inside a vacuum chamber, though batch furnaces mostly work without one. In the chamber, neutral fragments called radicals do most of the chemistry, and charged ions give etch its direction. In an extreme ultraviolet machine, a laser vaporizes droplets of molten tin into a plasma that gives off the 13.5-nanometer light.
   + Export controls describe each tool by the process it is designed to run. An extreme ultraviolet source has to make its tin plasma 50,000 times a second to give enough light for production.
   + Read it in Deposition and Etch Also in Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist, Lithography
   + polycrystalline silicon, electronic-grade polysilicon, semiconductor-grade polysilicon, polysilicon feedstock
   + Polysilicon is purified silicon that has not yet been grown into one crystal. It comes as gray rods made of many small crystals. It is the raw material that is melted down to pull the single crystal a wafer is cut from. The same material, at lower purity, goes into solar panels.
   + China made about 93 percent of the world's polysilicon in 2023. But the German wafer maker Siltronic names five major suppliers of the electronic-grade kind for 2025, and none of them is Chinese.
   + Read it in Silicon and Wafers
   + power shelves, rack power shelf
   + A power shelf is a slide-in unit in a rack that converts the incoming supply to the voltage the boards use and feeds it to the busbar. A GB300 NVL72 rack draws its 142 kilowatts through eight of them at 33 kilowatts each.
   + Many firms supply the shelves and the boards behind them. The large transformers upstream commonly took three years to arrive in 2024.
   + Important · Advanced packaging · Memory packaging and test
   + Specializes in memory assembly and test, including DRAM and NAND packaging, and has been adding capacity for HBM-related and logic advanced packaging work.
   + approval is presumed, presumed approval
   + A presumption of approval means a license is still required but will normally be granted. It is the opposite of a presumption of denial, and one rule can contain both. HBM license applications for Macau and Country Group D:5 are presumed approved when neither the buyer nor its ultimate parent is headquartered in either place, and presumed denied otherwise (15 CFR 742.6(b)(10)(ii)).
   + The split lets HBM reach plants in China owned by firms headquartered elsewhere while holding it back from Chinese-owned buyers.
   + A license review policy in which the reviewing agencies start from the position that an application should be refused. An application can still be filed and will be considered, but the exporter has to overcome that starting position. It is the general policy for advanced computing chips going to China and Macau under 15 CFR 742.6(b)(10), and for most Entity List entries. Since January 2026 export applications for some less powerful chips, such as the H200, are reviewed case by case instead.
   + The difference between a presumption of denial and a prohibition decides whether a company files at all. Under a presumption of denial an application can still be made, but under a prohibition it cannot.
   + Contested · Design & EDA · Device modeling, SPICE simulation and DTCO
   + A Chinese EDA vendor that makes device modeling, parameter extraction and fast SPICE circuit simulation software. Device models describe how the transistors on a foundry's process behave, so chip designers can simulate their circuits on that process.
   + PCB, PCBs, printed circuit boards, circuit board, circuit boards, high-multilayer PCB
   + A printed circuit board is the flat green or black card that electronic parts are soldered onto, with its wiring etched from copper sheets and stacked in layers. Boards for AI servers can run past 30 layers.
   + It carries the power and the links between GPUs. How much signal its laminate absorbs is now a main design limit.
   + Read it in Substrates and PCBs Also in Memory and HBM, Advanced Packaging, Test and Assembly, Systems and Networking, The Economics of AI Chips
   + Where it appears, 26 places
   + probe cards
   + A circuit board carrying thousands of fine needles that press onto the contact pads of a chip while it is still part of the wafer. A tester drives signals into the chip through the needles. Every card is custom-built for one chip design.
   + Because each card is custom-built and slow to qualify, probe card supply and lead time can limit how fast a new accelerator or memory stack reaches full production.
   + physical vapor deposition, sputtering, sputtering target, sputtering targets, sputter
   + Physical vapor deposition, or PVD, coats a wafer with metal in a vacuum. It knocks atoms off a solid slab of metal, the sputtering target, and they fly across and land on the wafer as a thin film. It lays down the metal barriers and seed layers under a chip's copper wiring.
   + Every copper wire needs that barrier, because copper seeps into the insulator around it. JX Advanced Metals puts its own share of the world market for semiconductor sputtering targets at about 65 percent as of fiscal 2026.

   + qualify, qualifies, qualified, qualifying, requalify, requalification, qualification tests, qualification lock-in
   + Qualification is the testing a fab or a customer runs before it will accept a new material, tool or supplier. A resist, a slurry, a wafer source or a memory stack is approved for a set use, such as one layer in one fab's process, and switching means running the tests again.
   + Even a resist that passes the first time takes seven to 17 months to approve, the Semiconductor Industry Association wrote in 2023, so a second source is slow to add.

   + cabinet, racks, cabinets, rack-scale
   + The standard steel frame that computing equipment is bolted into in a data center, roughly the size of a wardrobe. For AI, a whole rack is wired and sold as one machine, so the rack itself is the product.
   + A GB300 NVL72 rack draws up to 142 kilowatts. Nvidia's 800-volt power design is intended for racks of more than a megawatt from 2027. A megawatt rack needs a different busbar, the metal bar that carries power to its equipment. It also changes the weight the floor must bear, the coolant flow and the energy stored in the rack to keep its power draw steady.

   + ramps, ramp-up, ramping, yield at ramp
   + A ramp is the months in which a new process or product climbs from a few wafers a week to full output while the share of working chips rises. Yield is lowest at the start of the ramp and improves as engineers find which steps lose dies.
   + Whether a new process makes a profit or needs a subsidy depends on its yield in the first year.

   + Beijing put seven of them under export license in April 2025. In October 2025 it claimed jurisdiction over foreign goods containing Chinese rare earths worth 0.1 percent or more of their value. That rule is suspended until 10 November 2026.

   + RDL, redistribution wiring, redistribution layers
   + A redistribution layer is a thin sheet of fine copper wiring, laid down in resin, that spreads a die's tightly spaced connections out to a wider pattern the package can handle. CoWoS-R and CoWoS-L use it in place of a full silicon interposer.
   + TSMC pushed packages past 3.3 times the reticle size by swapping silicon for redistribution wiring.

   + Shipping an item that falls under US export rules, the EAR, from one foreign country to another. The term is defined at 15 CFR 734.14. A memory stack subject to the EAR moving from Korea to China is a reexport, and it needs the same US license a shipment from Texas would.
   + The reexport rule lets US controls follow an item after it leaves the United States, so they reach trade between two foreign countries.

   + One exposure from a reticle prints at most 26 by 33 millimeters of the wafer, and every large AI chip has to be designed around that limit.

   + reticle size limit, reticle field, field size, reticle-limited, reticle size, reticles, times reticle
   + The largest chip a lithography machine can print in a single exposure, about 858 square millimeters, or 26 by 33 millimeters. It is set by the machine's optics and is the same for every design. The newer High-NA EUV machines print half that area.
   + Many leading AI accelerators, Nvidia's Blackwell among them, need more circuitry than this, so they are split into several dies, separate pieces of silicon, and joined back together inside a package.
   + Read it in Advanced Packaging Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, The Economics of AI Chips
   + RISC-V cores, RISC-V processor
   + RISC-V is an open instruction set, the list of basic commands a processor understands, that anyone may use without a license. Companies build their own processor cores on it, and Nvidia now uses such cores for small control tasks inside every GPU.
   + It is the license-free alternative to Arm's processor designs.
   + register-transfer level, register-transfer-level, register transfer level, RTL description, RTL code
   + RTL, for register-transfer level, is the code in which a chip is first written. It is text, in a language such as SystemVerilog, that describes what every storage element holds on every tick of the clock. Software later turns it into transistors and wires.
   + It defines what the chip does. A large team checks it in simulation and emulation before tape-out, because a mistake found later needs new masks.

   + back-end network, cluster fabric, scale-out, scale out
   + The network that joins thousands of accelerator groups into one cluster, running at hundreds of gigabits per second over Ethernet or InfiniBand. Its Ethernet versions use the same standard as ordinary data center networks.
   + It lets thousands of accelerators train one model together. InfiniBand was the main scale-out network when AI clusters were small. But Ethernet capacity has since grown quickly, as Broadcom doubled the bandwidth of its switch chips every generation.
   + scale-up fabric, coherent domain, scale-up, scale up, scale-up domain, scale-up switch
   + The network that joins a group of accelerators, from a few dozen in one rack to thousands, so a model too large for a single chip can be spread across them. It runs at terabytes per second. In Nvidia's GB300 NVL72 each GPU has nine times as much bandwidth on it as on the scale-out network.
   + The bandwidth of this network limits how efficiently a large model trains. Nvidia sells its own NVLink for it, and UALink is a published open standard that competes with NVLink.
   + scanners, stepper, steppers, lithography scanner, lithography scanners, lithography machine, lithography system, lithography systems
   + A scanner is the lithography machine itself. It holds the photomask up to a light source and projects a shrunken image of one layer onto the wafer. Mask and wafer sweep past each other in a narrow slit of light. An older stepper exposes the whole mask image at once instead of scanning. A standard EUV scanner sells for around $200 million.
   + ASML makes every EUV scanner in the world, so a rule aimed at its scanners reaches all advanced chipmaking.

   + second sources, second-source, multi-sourcing, dual source, single source, sole source, single-source

   + serializer-deserializer, high-speed serial links, serial links, serial link
   + A SerDes, for serializer-deserializer, is the circuit at the edge of a chip that turns many slow parallel signals into one very fast stream over a single pair of wires. A SerDes at the other end turns the stream back into parallel signals. Every link out of an accelerator, to another chip, a switch or an optical module, runs through one.
   + SerDes speed sets how much data a chip can move. Many chip designers license the blocks as IP, and each design has to be proven in silicon on every new process.

   + sign-off, static timing analysis, timing signoff, timing sign-off, physical verification, signed off
   + Signoff is the final set of automated checks a chip design must pass before tape-out. Static timing analysis checks that every signal arrives in time at the clock speed promised. Physical verification checks that every shape in the layout can be printed.
   + A foundry accepts only layouts that pass physical verification against its rules. At advanced nodes, Synopsys holds more than 90 percent of timing signoff, and Siemens more than 85 percent of physical verification.

   + silicon bridges, EMIB, silicon-bridge, local silicon bridges, embedded bridge
   + A silicon bridge is a small sliver of silicon carrying dense wiring. It is set into the package only where two dies need thousands of connections between them, so the rest of the package can use cheaper wiring. Intel calls its version EMIB, and TSMC's CoWoS-L uses the same idea.
   + Bridges let a package grow past a single interposer's size limit, and CoWoS-L uses them to package Nvidia's Blackwell and Rubin chips. The bridges in Blackwell needed very high placement accuracy, and packaging issues limited its supply.

   + specialty gas, electronic specialty gases, bulk gases, bulk gas, electronic gases, process gas, process gases
   + Specialty gases are the gases a fab uses in small amounts, each for one job. Silane, for example, carries silicon into the chamber to build a film, and nitrogen trifluoride cleans the chamber. They arrive in cylinders. Bulk gases, such as nitrogen and oxygen, come by pipeline or from plants built at the fab.
   + Specialty gases are shipped to the fab in cylinders, so a government or a war can cut them off. Ukraine supplied about 70 percent of the world's neon before Russia invaded in 2022.
   + static random-access memory, cache, on-chip memory, on-die SRAM, on-die memory, L2 cache
   + SRAM is the fast memory built from transistors on the logic chip itself, used as a cache that holds the numbers a processor is about to need. It is far faster than the memory stacks beside the chip and holds far less.
   + The cell that holds one bit has barely shrunk since the 5 nm node, which limits how much cache a designer can afford to put on an accelerator.
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Advanced Packaging
   + Critical · Chemicals & gases · Ultra-high-purity hydrofluoric acid
   + Refines hydrofluoric acid to the purity needed to etch and clean silicon without leaving metal contamination. Japan's 2019 decision to require export licenses for high-purity HF sent to Korea showed how few qualified sources exist.
   + The test for whether an item falls under US export rules at all. Under 15 CFR 734.3 it covers anything in the United States and anything of US origin wherever it is. It also covers foreign-made goods that contain more than a permitted share of controlled US content, and foreign-made goods caught by a foreign direct product rule. The test applies to the item, whatever the seller's nationality.
   + The rules follow the item, so a Dutch or Korean firm shipping between two foreign countries can still need a US license.
   + substitutability rating
   + A four-band rating of how hard it would be for a well-funded state or firm, starting today, to build an alternative to the leading suppliers at one stage of the supply chain. It is judged against four written tests. Easy: a second supplier is qualified, meaning approved by customers, and shipping today at the leading node, the most advanced process. Moderate: a second supplier exists but is not yet qualified at the leading node, and closing the gap takes about two to five years. Hard: no second supplier exists at the leading node, the path to one is known, and closing the gap takes five to ten years. Very hard: no second supplier exists, and the path depends on physics, decades of unwritten know-how or protected intellectual property. No one has yet repeated what the leaders did, and closing the gap takes ten years or more. The years are always counted for that same well-funded state or firm, and the count stops at qualification, which comes well after a prototype.
   + It says how long a rival or a government would need to replace a stage. The debate over export controls turns on that question.
   + package substrate, IC substrate, substrates, build-up substrate, build-up substrates, package substrates, IC substrates, resin substrate
   + The small, dense circuit board at the base of a chip package. The chips sit on it, and it spreads their microscopic connections out to the millimeter-scale solder balls a motherboard can accept. It is built up layer by layer from epoxy film, laser-drilled holes and plated copper.
   + A handful of firms, most of them in Asia, build these substrates for others. As packages grow, a substrate warps more and needs more layers, so fewer come out working.
   + Read it in Substrates and PCBs Also in Advanced Packaging, Test and Assembly, The Economics of AI Chips

   + Makes the synthesis, place-and-route, verification and signoff software that turns the code describing an AI accelerator's logic (its RTL) into a layout a fab can build. It also sells the largest commercial catalog of silicon IP, ready-made circuit blocks that chip designers license. Its 2025 acquisition of Ansys added multiphysics simulation for thermal and power integrity.
   + Taiwan
   + TWh
   + 24 entries
   + leads 4 stages, 3 of them extreme · named on 10 of 15 chokepoint cards · 25 firms in the supplier list, 1 Critical
   + Makes nearly every leading-edge chip and packages most of them.
   + Card and 94 places
   + tape out, taped out, tapes out, tape-outs, tapeout
   + The moment a chip design is declared finished and the pattern files are sent to the factory to be written into masks. The name survives from when the data physically left on a reel of magnetic tape.
   + Each revision after tape-out, called a respin, needs at least some new masks. In May 2026 SemiAnalysis put a single respin at leading-edge nodes at $50 million to $100 million, so designers check everything they can before tape-out.
   + MR-MUF, Advanced MR-MUF, TC-NCF bonding
   + TC-NCF and MR-MUF are two ways of joining the thinned memory chips in an HBM stack. In TC-NCF, thermocompression with non-conductive film, each chip is pressed onto the one below under heat, with a thin insulating film between them. Samsung uses it for its twelve-high HBM3E. In MR-MUF, used by SK hynix, the chips are stacked first, and a liquid protective material is injected between them and hardened.
   + Thinner chips and taller stacks warp more easily, and both methods aim to control that. SK hynix says its Advanced MR-MUF also dissipates heat well.
   + firm, Italy
   + Important · Test · Probe cards and probe heads
   + Makes probe cards, specializing in vertical MEMS probe heads used for high pin count logic and HBM test. It is the main European probe card maker and the closest competitor to FormFactor.
   + Contested · Accelerator vendors · RISC-V AI processors and licensable IP
   + Builds AI accelerators around RISC-V cores, with Ethernet links that join many chips into one system. It also licenses the designs as IP so customers can build their own chips.
   + Important · Test · Automated test equipment
   + Supplies SoC, memory and system-level test platforms, and forms the automated test equipment duopoly with Advantest. It also sells industrial automation equipment through its robotics arm.
   + thermocompression, thermo-compression bonding, thermocompression bonder, thermocompression bonders, TCB, die bonder, die bonders, bonder, bonders
   + Thermocompression bonding presses one die onto another, or onto a substrate, under heat and force until the tiny solder bumps between them melt and join. It is how memory dies are stacked to make high bandwidth memory (HBM) and a growing way to place chiplets on a package.
   + ASMPT says it remained the leader in these bonders in 2025, when its revenue from them rose about 146 percent. It aims for 35 to 40 percent of a market it puts at $1.6 billion in 2028.
   + wafers an hour, wafers per hour, wafers-per-hour
   + Throughput is how many wafers a machine can process in an hour. ASML's NXE:3800E EUV scanner reaches 220.
   + Throughput sets the cost of each layer and how many lots, or batches of wafers, a fab can afford to inspect. Brighter EUV light sources and multi-beam inspection tools both aim to raise it.
   + Read it in Lithography Also in Metrology and Inspection
   + Important · Silicon wafers · Semiconductor-grade polysilicon and IPA
   + Japanese chemical maker supplying semiconductor-grade polysilicon from Shunan and Malaysia, plus high-purity isopropyl alcohol and other fab chemicals. It is one of the five major suppliers of electronic-grade silicon that Siltronic names for 2025.
   + Critical · Deposition & etch · Coater/developer track, etch, deposition and clean
   + Makes the coater/developer track, which applies photoresist, develops it after exposure and is physically linked to every scanner in a fab. It also makes etch, deposition, cleaning and wafer bonding systems, and wafer probers for chip testing.
   + Card and 15 places
   + Important · Photoresist · Photoresist and high-purity process chemicals
   + Makes photoresist for KrF, ArF immersion and EUV lithography, plus developers, strippers and thick-film resists used in advanced packaging. It has supplied resist since the industry's early days.
   + Important · Test · Wafer probers, dicing machines and grinders
   + Makes probers, the machines that move each wafer under the probe card and hold it at a set temperature during wafer test, plus dicing machines and grinders. It sells as Accretech and says it has the top global share in probers.
   + Contested · Advanced packaging · OSAT assembly and test, AMD partner
   + A Chinese OSAT that packages a large share of AMD's CPUs and GPUs through joint venture plants acquired from AMD in 2016. It is developing 2.5D chiplet packaging.
   + Important · Photomasks · Advanced photomasks and FC-BGA substrates
   + Makes advanced photomasks through its photomask arm and is building flip-chip BGA substrate capacity for AI packages. Its photomask business became a separate company, Tekscend Photomask, which is listed in Tokyo.
   + Important · Foundry · Specialty analog and silicon photonics foundry
   + A specialty foundry for analog, RF, power and silicon photonics, including the photonic integrated circuits used in optical transceivers and co-packaged optics.
   + total processing performance
   + Total processing performance, the measure US export rules use for a chip's computing power. It starts from the number of multiply-accumulate operations, a multiply followed by an add, that the chip does each second. That number is doubled, multiplied by the number of bits in each operation, and added up across every processing unit on the chip. The definition is in ECCN 3A090, in supplement no. 1 to 15 CFR part 774.
   + The export rules for chip sales to China draw their lines in TPP. The top band, 3A090.a, starts at 4,800 TPP, or at 1,600 TPP with a performance density of 5.92. Since 15 January 2026, export applications for chips below 21,000 TPP and 6,500 GB/s of total DRAM bandwidth, such as the H200, are reviewed case by case for end users in China and Macau (91 FR 1684).
   + Tensor Processing Unit, TPUs
   + Google's own AI chip, designed in-house for its data centers and cloud service. Its eighth generation, announced in April 2026, is split into two chips, the TPU 8t for training and the TPU 8i for inference.
   + It shows that a large buyer can design its own alternative to chips sold on the open market. Ironwood, the seventh generation, carries 192 GB of memory per chip, six times its predecessor's. Google says the TPU 8t and TPU 8i deliver up to twice Ironwood's performance per watt.
   + Read it in Chip Design, EDA and IP Also in Advanced Packaging, Systems and Networking, Data Centers and Power
   + transistors
   + The switch every chip is built from. Current runs through a channel between two terminals. A voltage on a third terminal, the gate, opens or closes that path, much as a tap opens or closes a pipe. One Nvidia Blackwell part contains 208 billion of them.
   + The whole chip supply chain exists to make transistors smaller, faster and less leaky. Only three companies, TSMC, Intel and Samsung, make the newest kind in volume.
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End Also in Chip Design, EDA and IP, Photomasks and Pellicles, Who Controls the Chain
   + logic density, chip density, transistors per square millimeter, density gain
   + Transistor density is how many transistors fit in a square millimeter of chip. SemiAnalysis measured SMIC's N+3 at 113.4 million, against 107.7 million for TSMC's N6. TSMC says N2P gives 1.2 times the logic density and 1.15 times the chip density of N3E. A16, which adds backside power, gives up to 1.10 times the chip density of N2P. TSMC says A14, planned for 2028, will give more than 20 percent more logic density than N2.
   + Density sets how much logic fits on a die of a given size. No foundry publishes wafer prices, so cost per transistor cannot be worked out from public figures.
   + Read it in Transistors and the Front End Also in Who Controls the Chain
   + Critical · Equipment subsystems · CO2 drive lasers for EUV sources
   + Builds the multi-kilowatt CO2 laser amplifier chain that vaporizes tin droplets to create EUV light. Trumpf describes the laser system as internationally unique.
   + Critical · Foundry · Leading-edge logic foundry and advanced packaging
   + Manufactures almost every leading-edge AI accelerator, including those of Nvidia, AMD and Broadcom and the custom chips of large cloud companies, on its N4, N3 and N2 processes. It also owns CoWoS and SoIC advanced packaging, and its packaging capacity is a tighter constraint than its wafer capacity.
   + Card and 129 places
   + through-silicon via, TSVs, through-silicon vias, through silicon via, through silicon vias
   + A copper column that runs all the way through a chip so that power and signals can pass from its top face to its bottom face. It makes stacking chips vertically possible, in the way an elevator shaft makes a tall building usable.
   + High bandwidth memory depends on these columns. US rules control the tools that cut the holes for them, and define those tools by hole shape and etching speed.
   + Read it in Memory and HBM Also in Deposition and Etch
   + Important · Substrates & PCBs · PCBs and RF components
   + Makes printed circuit boards for data center, aerospace and defense customers, including high-layer-count boards for AI and networking systems. By its own description it is one of the largest technology manufacturers in North America. It also offers IC substrates and is building an ultra-high-density circuit board plant in Syracuse, New York, with volume production expected in the second half of 2026.
   + terawatt-hour, terawatt-hours, terawatt hours
   + A terawatt-hour is a billion kilowatt-hours, the unit national electricity use is counted in. US data centers used 192 TWh in 2024, 4.7 percent of the country's electricity.
   + Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory's reference case projects 649 TWh in 2030, 11.8 percent of forecast US electricity use, within a range of 521 to 843 TWh.
   + Read it in Data Centers and Power Also in The Economics of AI Chips
   + UCIe
   + utilization
   + Universal Chiplet Interconnect Express, UCIe 3.0
   + UCIe is an open standard for the short, very fast link between two chiplets sitting side by side in one package. It lets chiplets from different vendors be wired together, the way PCIe lets cards from different vendors share a computer.
   + Accelerators too large to print as one chip are built from chiplets, and a shared standard lets more firms supply the pieces.
   + Important · Equipment subsystems · Subsystem assembly and parts cleaning
   + Builds gas delivery systems, chemical modules and frame assemblies for process tools, and runs a large business cleaning and coating chamber parts between uses.
   + Diversified · Foundry · Mature-node foundry
   + Taiwan's second largest foundry, running processes of 28 nm and above for display drivers, power management, connectivity and automotive parts. It is expanding 12 nm capacity in partnership with Intel.
   + Important · Substrates & PCBs · IC substrates and HDI boards
   + One of the largest IC substrate makers, producing ABF and BT substrates plus high density interconnect boards. It is adding ABF capacity in Taiwan and Shenzhen for AI demand.
   + named on 1 of 15 chokepoint cards · 5 firms in the supplier list, 1 Critical
   + Arm and the other processor designs that most chips license.
   + Card and 56 places
   + leads 5 stages, 1 of them extreme · named on 14 of 15 chokepoint cards · 67 firms in the supplier list, 7 Critical
   + Designs the chips, makes most of the tools and is the biggest buyer.
   + Card and 263 places
   + US persons, U.S. person, U.S. persons
   + In export law a US person is any American citizen, permanent resident or company, wherever in the world they are, and anyone in the United States. Since October 2022 a US person may not service or support an advanced chip fab in China without a license.
   + The rule reaches people as well as goods, so American citizens, residents and companies need a license to support advanced chipmaking in China.
   + Read it in Who Controls the Chain Also in Deposition and Etch, Systems and Networking
   + Diversified · Advanced packaging · OSAT assembly and test
   + A Singapore-based OSAT providing assembly and test for analog, mixed-signal, automotive and memory devices from plants across Southeast Asia and China.
   + utilization rate, fab utilization
   + Utilization is how full a fab's lines are running, as a share of what they could run. Most of a fab's costs, such as paying off its tools, stay the same whether it runs full or half empty, so utilization largely decides whether it makes money.
   + SMIC ran its lines 93.5 percent full in 2025, and its gross margin that year was 21 percent.
   + VAT Group
   + Victory Giant Technology
   + 6 entries
   + Critical · Equipment subsystems · High-vacuum valves
   + Makes the high-vacuum isolation and control valves that seal and gate every process chamber. How many particles a valve sheds and how many cycles it lasts directly affect tool uptime and wafer defect counts.
   + Important · Equipment subsystems · Precision mechanical modules and frames
   + A Dutch precision manufacturing group that builds large mechanical modules, frames and vacuum assemblies for ASML scanners. It is one of the main suppliers in the Brainport cluster around ASML's base in Veldhoven.
   + Important · Power & cooling · Data center power and thermal management
   + Makes uninterruptible power supplies, busway power distribution, coolant distribution units and liquid cooling systems for data centers. It co-develops reference power and cooling designs with Nvidia.
   + Validated End-User, Validated End User, Validated End-User status, VEU status, VEU cover, validated end users
   + An authorization that BIS, the US Commerce Department office that licenses exports, grants to a named foreign facility, so approved items can ship to it without an individual license each time. The approved facilities are listed in supplement no. 7 to 15 CFR part 748, and the procedure is set out in 15 CFR 748.15.
   + It let the China subsidiaries of Samsung and SK hynix, and the Dalian NAND flash memory fab that SK hynix bought from Intel, receive US equipment without individual licenses. BIS revoked that status effective 31 December 2025 (90 FR 42321). It said it would license those fabs to keep running, but not to expand capacity or upgrade technology (90 FR 42321). In December 2025 BIS granted SK hynix an annual license to ship approved US equipment to its China fabs during 2026.
   + Read it in Who Controls the Chain Also in Deposition and Etch
   + copper vias, vertical vias, vertical copper vias
   + Vias are the small vertical holes, filled with metal, that connect one layer of wiring to the layer above or below it. A chip's wiring is full of them. An interposer has them through its whole thickness, and a package substrate has them drilled by laser through each sheet of film.
   + Every added layer brings another set of vias that can fail, so yield falls as packages and substrates grow.
   + Read it in Advanced Packaging Also in Deposition and Etch, Memory and HBM
   + Contested · Substrates & PCBs · High-multilayer PCBs for AI servers
   + Makes high-layer-count printed circuit boards and supplies AI accelerator baseboards and switch boards to the server ODMs.
   + Wacker Chemie
   + WUS Printed Circuit
   + 11 entries
   + Important · Silicon wafers · Semiconductor-grade polysilicon
   + One of the few producers of semiconductor-grade polysilicon, which it makes at Burghausen in Germany and in Tennessee. Its silicone and chemicals businesses bring in much more revenue than its electronic-grade polysilicon.
   + silicon wafer, 300 mm wafer, wafers, silicon wafers, 300 mm wafers
   + The polished disc of ultrapure silicon that chips are built on, 300 millimeters across for advanced chips. Hundreds of chips are printed on one wafer and cut apart at the end.
   + A flaw wastes more silicon on a large AI chip than on a small one, so AI chips need wafers that are nearly flawless and flat to the edge. The top five wafer makers hold around 75 percent of a market worth $11.4 billion in 2025.
   + Read it in Silicon and Wafers Also in Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist, Lithography, Photomasks and Pellicles, Deposition and Etch, Metrology and Inspection, Transistors and the Front End, Foundries and Fabs, Memory and HBM, Advanced Packaging, Test and Assembly, Data Centers and Power, The Economics of AI Chips, Who Controls the Chain
   + Where it appears, 164 places
   + WFE, front-end equipment, equipment billings, wafer processing equipment, semiconductor equipment, chipmaking tools, chipmaking equipment
   + The collective name for the machines that process wafers inside a fab: printing systems, deposition and etch chambers, cleaning tools and measurement systems. It excludes the packaging and test machines used afterward.
   + Spending on it shows how much new chipmaking capacity is being built. World sales of all chipmaking equipment reached $135.1 billion in 2025, and sales of wafer processing tools rose 12 percent.
   + Read it in Deposition and Etch Also in Metrology and Inspection, The Economics of AI Chips, Who Controls the Chain
   + wafer test, probe test, wafer probe
   + Wafer sort is the first test a chip gets, while it is still on the wafer. Thousands of needles on a probe card press onto the pads of one die, or of several dies at once, and a tester drives signals in and reads the answers. Any die that answers wrong is marked and never packaged.
   + Proving a die good before it is packaged keeps one bad die from scrapping the memory stacks and substrate it would be joined to.
   + wafers a month, wafer starts, wafer starts per month, WSPM, wafer starts a month, million wafers per month
   + Wafers per month is how fab capacity is counted: the number of wafers a plant can start through its line each month. A TSMC gigafab runs more than 100,000. In September 2025 SemiAnalysis conservatively put SMIC's capacity at 7 nm and below at 45,000 a month by the end of 2025. Figures are often converted to 8-inch or 12-inch equivalents so plants with different wafer sizes can be compared.
   + In June 2024 the industry association SEMI forecast China at 10.1 million wafers a month in 2025, the most of any region. In 2026 it projected China would stay largest through 2027, but little of that capacity is advanced.
   + Read it in Foundries and Fabs Also in Silicon and Wafers, Deposition and Etch, Advanced Packaging, Who Controls the Chain
   + wet chemical, wet processing, wet clean, cleaning chemicals, process chemicals
   + Wet chemicals are the liquids a wafer is dipped in or sprayed with between steps: acids that strip a layer, solvents that remove resist, and ultrapure water rinses. Wet processing is any step done in liquid.
   + CSET's January 2021 brief, drawing on 2019 market reports, put five firms at more than 60 percent of the wet chemicals market. BIS controls wet processing tools by how selectively they etch one material over another.
   + Diversified · Memory · Specialty DRAM, NOR flash and CUBE memory
   + Makes NOR flash, specialty low-power DRAM and a customized ultra-bandwidth memory called CUBE aimed at edge AI devices that cannot afford HBM.
   + Important · Systems & servers · AI server and GPU baseboard manufacturing
   + Builds GPU baseboards and AI servers, and grew quickly during the 2025 AI build-out. It also supplies liquid cooling loops and rack integration.
   + Important · Systems & servers · Hyperscale server and rack ODM
   + Designs and builds hyperscale servers, storage and rack systems to Open Compute designs for cloud operators, including liquid-cooled AI racks.
   + Contested · Metrology · Domestic Chinese metrology and display test
   + Makes semiconductor film metrology, electrical test and defect inspection tools for Chinese fabs through its Wuhan Jingce Semiconductor arm. It started in flat panel display test.
   + Contested · Substrates & PCBs · High-speed PCBs for servers and networking
   + Makes high-speed multilayer printed circuit boards for servers, switches and AI accelerators, and is a main supplier to networking equipment makers.
   + yield
   + YMTC
   + 2 entries
   + die yield, yields, packaging yield, package yield, stack yield
   + The percentage of chips on a wafer that work. Flaws land at random, so the larger the chip the more likely it is to catch one, and yield falls steeply as chips get bigger.
   + Yield sets the cost of a working AI chip. Epoch AI models the Blackwell compute die at 40 to 70 percent good, with 60 percent as its central estimate. In the same model, the cost of packages that fail testing, spread across the ones that pass, adds about $1,000 to each B200.
   + Read it in The Economics of AI Chips Also in Chemicals, Gases and Photoresist, Deposition and Etch, Metrology and Inspection, Transistors and the Front End, Advanced Packaging, Substrates and PCBs, Who Controls the Chain
   + Where it appears, 25 places
   + Contested · Memory · Chinese 3D NAND
   + China's 3D NAND maker, using its Xtacking architecture that bonds a separately made logic wafer onto the memory array. It has been on the US Entity List since 2022.
   + ZT Systems
   + 1 entry
   + Important · Systems & servers · Rack-scale AI system design
   + Designs and validates rack-scale AI systems for hyperscalers. AMD bought it for its systems engineering team, so that AMD could ship complete racks.
